Item insert stamps both routing defaults on every new item; existing items re-uploaded with a different sub-container retain their existing defaults (no silent overwrite). **Dialog:** Download Template is now async — fetches existing categories + sub-containers before workbook generation, shows "Building…" spinner. Preview table adds a Sub-container column with division badge; Category Path renders segments joined with " › ". Verified on staging (5 active sub-containers).
- [2026-08-02] **Phase D.12 Task 5: COGS routing to consumer division** — 3 migrations (`20260802000800_phase_d12_cogs_consumer_division.sql`, `_000900_get_warehouse_names_rpc.sql`, `_001000_sub_container_lookup_and_create_delivery.sql`), `src/hooks/useDeliveryLineItemShares.ts` (new), `src/hooks/useSaleOrders.ts`, `src/hooks/useWarehouseOperations.ts`, `src/components/sales/SoDeliveryDialog.tsx`, `src/components/sales/DeliveryFormDialog.tsx`, `src/types/database.types.ts`. Cross-division consumer accounting: when a division sells from another division's shared stock, COGS books to the SO's division (the consumer), not the physical owner. **DB:** `cogs_entries.consumer_division_id uuid` FK `company_divisions` + index + backfill from legacy `division_id` (already SO's division via `set_division_from_sale_order` trigger); new `set_consumer_division_from_sale_order` trigger keeps both columns in lockstep on new inserts; `complete_delivery_inventory` rewritten so the cross-division sub-container guard permits delivery IFF every delivered line's item has SO's division in its `shared_with_division_ids` (unshared lines still raise with an updated error message naming the count) and stamps `consumer_division_id = SO's division_id` on every COGS insert while legacy `division_id` continues to receive the same value via the existing trigger (all P&L / cogs summary reads keep working); `create_and_confirm_delivery` gains 6th arg `p_sub_container_id` (DEFAULT NULL) threaded through to `complete_delivery_inventory`; two new SECURITY DEFINER RPCs `get_warehouse_names(p_ids uuid[])` and `get_warehouse_sub_containers(p_warehouse_id uuid)` so a Kitchen operator can resolve names and pick Maintenance sub-containers for a shared item without tripping the division-scoped RLS on `warehouses` / `warehouse_sub_containers`. **App:** `useDeliveryLineItemShares` batch-loads `shared_with_division_ids` per variant and exposes `allShared(divisionId)` so both delivery dialogs can decide when to relax the same-division sub filter. `SoDeliveryDialog` (create-pending) gains a Sub-container picker below Warehouse — auto-selects when 1 sub, select when >1, `(shared)` suffix on cross-division options, required when >1; Warehouse dropdown unions the RLS-scoped list with warehouses appearing in the stock map (name resolved via `get_warehouse_names`); stock badges scope to the picked sub-container when set (fell back to per-warehouse totals before selection) so delivered qty reflects what's physically available in the chosen sub; qty input protected from mouse-wheel value mutations. `DeliveryFormDialog` (complete-pending) — `eligibleSubs` now permits cross-division subs when `allShared(soDivisionId)`. `useSaleOrders.useCreateDelivery` forwards `sub_container_id`. `useWarehouseStockByItems` second-pass resolves warehouse names via `get_warehouse_names` for cross-division pools missing from the caller's `useWarehouses()` list. Regen'd `database.types.ts` for the three migrations; helper aliases re-appended. **Verified on staging** with SO-00019 (Kitchen consuming Maintenance's shared Split AC via Birkat Alawamer, delivery DEL-00033): `cogs_entries.consumer_division_id = Kitchen` ✓, legacy `division_id = Kitchen` (trigger-mirrored) ✓, `source_id → fifo_cost_layers.sub_container_id → warehouse_sub_containers.division_id = Maintenance` ✓ (physical trace preserved). Prod migrations still behind. D.12 phase fully shipped — Tasks 1–5 all complete.
- [2026-08-02] **Phase D.12 Task 4: Shared-from chip in cascade picker (item + variant rows)** — 1 new hook + 1 modified hook + 1 modified UI file (no migration). Sales-side cascade picker now surfaces cross-division sharing at two levels when `filterByActiveDivision` is on and an active division is set. **Item list:** items that are accessible only via a share (no owned stock in active division) get an amber "Shared" chip next to the name (`accessibility.accessibleItemIds.has(id) && !accessibility.ownedItemIds.has(id)`). **Variant popover:** each brand variant expands into one row per division holding stock; rows whose division !== active render an amber "Shared from `<Division>`" chip; rows on the active division show the division name and qty inline without a chip. Popover widened `w-56` → `w-72` to fit the extended row layout without wrapping. **Hook (`useVariantStockByDivision`):** fetches per-variant per-division stock for a single item. Two-step: variants for the item → `warehouse_stock_summary` with `sub_container_id(division_id, divisions:division_id(name))` join at `qty > 0`. Returns `Map<variant_id, VariantDivisionPool[]>` where each pool is `{ division_id, division_name, qty, reserved }` sorted by qty desc. `staleTime: 60 * 1000`. Only fires when the caller opts in (query key gated on `filterByActiveDivision ? selectedItem?.id : null` inside the cascade component) so purchase-side callers don't pay the roundtrip. **Hook (`useCascadeAccessibleItems`) extended:** now also exposes `ownedItemIds` so the item row can distinguish share-only items from owned ones; existing `accessibleItemIds` semantics unchanged. **Selection semantics unchanged:** clicking either pool of the same variant still calls `handleVariantSelect` with the variant_id only — division routing at the accounting layer (`consumer_division_id` on `cogs_entries` + delivery RPC) lands in Task 5. **Fallback:** when per-division breakdown isn't available (filter off, still loading, or variant has zero stock anywhere), variant popover renders the original single-row layout unchanged — purchase-side cascade and out-of-stock items keep their previous appearance. Operator confirmed on staging: Kitchen active → Split AC · Classic renders in item list with "Shared" chip; opening the variant popover shows the Witforms row with "Shared from Maintenance" chip + 65 avail. Maintenance active → same variant renders as `Witforms · Maintenance · 65 avail` with no chip. **Explicitly out of Task 4:** COGS routing (Task 5).

- [2026-08-02] **Phase D.12 Task 3: cascade picker division-aware filter + create-page division mirror** — 1 new hook + 4 modified UI files + 2 modified page files (no migration). Sales-side cascade pickers now prune to items the active division can legitimately consume: EITHER owns stock in a sub-container it controls (via `warehouse_sub_containers.division_id = activeDivisionId` + `warehouse_stock_summary.qty > 0`), OR the item's `shared_with_division_ids` contains the active division AND the item has stock somewhere in the caller's RLS scope. Categories with no matching items collapse recursively. **Hook (`useCascadeAccessibleItems`):** `type` + `activeDivisionId` + `enabled` scoped. Skips entirely when `enabled=false`, when `activeDivisionId` is null, or when `type='tools'` (no consumption path). Items query uses `inventory_categories!inner` type filter — no division dependency, cached across division switches. Stock lookup is two-step chunked: brand variants for the type's items → `warehouse_stock_summary` rows with `warehouse_sub_containers!inner(division_id)` join at `qty > 0`. Returns `{item_id, division_id}` pairs so the caller can classify owned vs anywhere. `staleTime: 5 * 60 * 1000` on both queries + `isLoading` gated on `queryIsLoading && !data` → repeat picker opens render the filtered tree instantly with silent background refetch. **Component (`CascadeInventorySelector`):** new `filterByActiveDivision?: boolean` prop (default `false`). When on, computes `visibleCategoryIds` by walking each accessible item's category up to root, then prunes the raw tree, item list, and L2/L3 options. L2/L3 options now derive from the **pruned** tree via id-lookup (`tree.find(n => n.id === selectedL1.id)?.children`) so the filter propagates down cascade levels correctly — earlier they used the raw `selectedL1.children` captured at selection time. **Opt-in callers (2):** `SoLineItemsEditor` (SO create/edit line rows), `ReplacementDeliveryDialog` (gift-item picker on sales returns). **Explicitly untouched (3):** `PoLineItemsEditor`, `PoReceiveTab`, `ReceivalFormDialog` — purchase/receival flows add stock, not consume it. **Create-page division mirror:** `/sales/create-so` and `/purchase/create-po` Division dropdowns now mirror the profile-menu `activeDivisionId` whenever it changes, not just on first mount. The earlier `if (divisionId) return` early-out was skipping subsequent syncs, so switching division in the profile menu mid-form left the visible Division field out of step with the cascade filter. New effect: always sync when active is in-scope, fallback to sole-division only when the user has just one AND nothing is picked yet. Operator confirmed on staging: Kitchen active → cascade tree collapses to accessible items only; Maintenance active → tree expands to Maintenance's full stock; profile-menu switch mid-form updates both the Division field AND the cascade tree in step. **Explicitly out of Task 3:** shared-from chip on cascade rows (Task 4), COGS routing (Task 5).

- [2026-08-02] **Phase D.12 Task 2: master-list filter chips (Owned by my division / Shared with me / Shared by my division)** — 1 new hook + 2 modified UI files (no migration). Chip row added above the `/master-data/inventory` category tree; each chip narrows the tree to only branches containing at least one matching item and filters lazy-loaded items inside expanded categories. **Hook (`useItemDivisionMembership`):** `type` + `activeDivisionId`-scoped hook returning `ownedIds`, `sharedWithMeIds`, `sharedByMeIds`, `itemCategoryMap`, and `isLoading`. Items query uses `inventory_categories!inner` type filter (no division dependency, cached across division switches). Owned-stock lookup is a two-step chunked query — brand variants for the type's items → `warehouse_stock_summary` rows filtered by `sub_container.division_id` — because the fragile 3-level PostgREST `.eq()` filter (`inventory_item_brand_variants.inventory_items.inventory_categories.type`) doesn't resolve reliably on the deeper join. `staleTime: 5 * 60 * 1000` on both queries; `isLoading` gated on `queryIsLoading && !data` so background refetches keep last-known counts visible — no `…` flash on tab switches or page revisits. Both queries chunk their `.in()` calls at 200 IDs to stay under Supabase's URL-length cap. **UI (`ItemsListView`):** chip row rendered below the toolbar, `activeDivisionId` gate hides the whole row for super-viewer "All divisions" mode. Active chip = blue-600 fill + white count pill; inactive = white bg with muted count pill. Chip selection computes a `filterItemIds` Set; a category-visibility Set is built by walking each matching item's category up to root so parent branches render down to the item. `filterTree` (search) composes with the division filter — search first, then division prune. Pagination + move handlers now use `divisionFilter` in the reset deps so page-1 snaps back on chip switch. **UI (`CategoryRow`):** accepts optional `filterItemIds` prop; when present, filters the lazy-loaded item list and passes the same set to recursive child rows. **Hooks race bug caught mid-testing:** an intermediate `useMemo` between the two `useQuery` calls caused a "Should have a queue" Fast-Refresh error when the hook file was patched with a new hook — moved the `itemIdsForType` computation inside the `queryFn` so the hook order stays stable across renders. Operator confirmed on staging: Kitchen active → Owned=0 (correct, no owned stock), Shared with me=1 (Split AC shared by Maintenance in Task 1), Shared by my division=0; Kitchen active on Spare Parts tab → Owned=1 (the Electrical spare part); Maintenance active → Owned=1, Shared by my division=1 (Split AC shared to Kitchen). Cache confirmed working: repeat navigation to the page shows counts instantly with no loading placeholder. **Explicitly out of Task 2:** cascade picker filter (Task 3), shared-from chip (Task 4), COGS routing (Task 5).

- [2026-08-01] **Phase D.12 Task 1: cross-division item sharing metadata + item-edit "Access & Sharing" section** — 1 migration (`20260801500000`) + 1 new hook + 1 modified UI file + types regen. **Schema:** `ALTER TABLE inventory_items ADD COLUMN shared_with_division_ids uuid[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` + GIN index (`idx_inventory_items_shared_with_division_ids`) for future `@>` lookups on the cascade picker. **No RLS change** — items are already globally visible via a permissive `USING (true)` policy for `authenticated`; the consumption gate lives on stock-table RLS via `is_sub_container_visible`, unaffected here. Column comment explains semantics + notes that the consumption path lands in later tasks. **Hook (`useItemStockByDivision`):** fetches the item's brand variants, aggregates `warehouse_stock_summary.qty` per division via the sub-container's `division_id` join, returns `Map<division_id, total_qty>`. Powers the "Owned · N" derivation in the item-edit panel. **UI (`ItemEditDialog`):** collapsible "Access & Sharing" section, default collapsed unless the item already has shares set. Header badge shows "Shared with N" on the collapsed panel when the saved array is non-empty. Per-division row rendering by state: (a) Owner (naturalQty > 0) — passive green-tinted row with "Owned · N" badge and **no checkbox** (they already have access, adding to `shared_with_division_ids` would be redundant/confusing — operator specifically requested this over the initial always-checkbox design); (b) Non-owner ticked — checkbox + "Shared" orange badge; (c) Non-owner unticked — checkbox + "no stock" grey text. Copy explains the model: items don't belong to a division, ownership is derived from whichever divisions physically hold stock, sharing adds access on top. Payload flows through the existing `useUpdateInventoryItem` mutation via the `shared_with_division_ids` field (types regen picked up the new column automatically). Types regen + DBTable/DBInsert/DBUpdate/AllTables helper aliases re-appended per the standing rule. Operator confirmed on staging: Split AC · Classic (65 units in Birkat Maintenance) renders Maintenance as `Owned · 65` passive green row; Kitchen renders as a tickable checkbox row. **Explicitly out of Task 1** (operator confirmed with the observation "SO and PO treat inventory the same" mid-testing — sharing has no consumption effect until the cascade picker filter ships): Task 2 (master-list filter chips), Task 3 (cascade picker division-aware filter), Task 4 ("Shared from `<div>`" chip on cascade), Task 5 (COGS routing via internal transfer journal at FIFO cost). Category tree question — categories travel with the item automatically because they're global (`inventory_items.category_id` → `inventory_categories`), so a shared Split AC will appear under Air Deflector > Classic > Split AC in the sharing division's cascade unchanged. Session handover doc at repo root `HANDOVER.md` captures the full Task 2–5 breakdown.

- [2026-08-01] **Flow Visualizer Phase B: static example fixtures per flow** — 1 new fixture file + generator extension + 5 seed examples. Operator chose the "static example fixtures" interpretation over the originally-proposed "live simulation with seed data" (which would have required a backend + auth + RLS + transaction rollback strategy — outside the "standalone HTML in docs/" scope Phase A established). **Fixture format:** `docs/flow-examples.json` keyed by flow slug (same `slugify(title)` the parser uses). Each fixture: `input` (operator payload), `writes` (array of `{ table, action, columns?, notes? }`), `ui_after` (bullet list of screen changes). Missing fixtures render no Example section — opt-in per flow. **Generator (`scripts/build-flows-visualizer.mjs`) extensions:** loads the JSON alongside the registry with graceful fallback (missing file → Phase A behavior); parses JSON errors with a clean stderr + `exit 1` rather than emitting broken HTML; warns at generation time about orphan fixture slugs that don't match any registered flow (typo guard). **UI additions:** blue "Example" badge next to the Status badge in the card header for flows with fixtures; collapsible `<details>` block below the field grid — no JS needed for expand/collapse (native browser); input pretty-printed as JSON in a monospace block; each write rendered as a left-bordered card with table name + action pill + columns as key/value grid + optional italic notes line; UI-after as a plain `<ul>` bullet list; new "With examples" filter chip at the top of the page (position 2 in the chip row). **CSS additions:** ~90 lines for `.ex-details`, `.ex-summary`, `.ex-write`, `.ex-cols`, `.ex-ui`, `.ex-json`, `.badge-example`, dark-mode aware, matches the existing card styling. **JS additions:** stream-filter chip logic extended to recognise `__examples__` as a valid `activeSection` value (`hasExample = card.dataset.hasExample === '1'`). **Seed with 5 examples covering different modules:** `create-sales-return-direct` (dual-ledger counters), `complete-delivery-confirm-book-stock` (FIFO deduction + cogs + summary refresh trigger), `send-damaged-for-repair` (full damaged-side chain including disposition link-back), `record-customer-payment` (invoice_payments allocation + FX trigger for foreign-currency SOs), `create-warehouse-transfer` (allocation reservation without physical movement). **To add more:** edit `docs/flow-examples.json`, add a top-level key = `slugify(flow title)`, fill in `{ input, writes[], ui_after[] }`, run `node scripts/build-flows-visualizer.mjs`; typo warnings surface if the slug is wrong. **Not in Phase B:** live DB simulation (requires backend + auth + RLS + transaction rollback strategy — deliberately out of scope per the "standalone HTML" architecture the operator chose).

- [2026-08-01] **Flow Visualizer Phase A: parse `docs/flows-registry.md` → self-contained `docs/flows-visualizer.html`** — 1 new script + 1 generated artifact + 1 .gitignore whitelist entry. Operator asked for the visualizer as a standalone HTML file inside `docs/` rather than a Next.js route (originally proposed `/dev/flows` — pivoted mid-planning). `scripts/build-flows-visualizer.mjs` reads the registry, parses every entry into structured fields, and generates a self-contained HTML page that opens offline in any browser. **Parser:** splits by top-level `##` sections; skips meta headers (Why this exists, Mandatory rule, Entry template, Cross-flow patterns, Registered placeholders); per section, splits by `### Flow Title` and parses the bullet list underneath. Registry template writes `- **Field:** value` with the colon INSIDE the bold — first-pass parser missed this and labels came out as `trigger_`, `hook_`, etc. Fix: strip trailing colon on the label before alias lookup. Field aliases normalise the many equivalent labels (Trigger surface(s) → trigger, Primary hook(s) → hook, Ledger writes → writes, etc.). Multi-line values supported: value extends until the next bullet or section separator. Unknown fields still render as red-labeled "extra" rows so future template drift is visible instead of silently dropped. **Table-hop extraction:** matches backticked identifiers that look like snake_case tables OR `table.column` pairs (captures only the table part — second-pass fix; first version missed all `so_invoices.payment_status`-style references because the dot broke the regex). Aggressive noise filter drops enum values, column-name lookalikes (fields ending `_id`/`_by`/`_at`, starting `p_`/`v_`/`rpc_`/`trg_`/`_`), and a skiplist of ~40 known non-table identifiers (status/enum/vocabulary names). **HTML output:** sticky top bar with brand + fuzzy search (searches title + full-text of each card); section-scoped filter chips (All + one per parsed section); sticky left sidebar TOC grouped by section with per-section counts; per-flow expandable card — title + status badge, section, "table hops" strip (chips joined by arrows), then the full ordered field list; wikilinks `[[Flow Name]]` resolve to anchors, unresolved ones grey out with a "no matching flow registered" tooltip; dark-mode aware via `prefers-color-scheme`; mobile-responsive (sidebar collapses under 900px, two-column field grid collapses at 700px); deep-linkable via `#flow-<slug>`. **.gitignore:** `/scripts/*` is ignored by default with a small whitelist; visualizer added next to `build_final_schema.mjs` and `build_test_workbook.mjs` since it's a repeat-use tool. **Ships:** 19 sections, 103 flows parsed from the current registry. Regenerate whenever `flows-registry.md` changes: `node scripts/build-flows-visualizer.mjs`. **Not in Phase A** (deferred to Phase B if pursued): mermaid-style directional diagrams (chip lists are enough for "which tables get touched"), live simulation with seed data, before/after diffs.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.13: unify damaged-stock movements into Warehouses → Movements + drop Damaged Stock Movements tab** — 3 code files (no migration). The Warehouses → Movements tab now surfaces the union of `inventory_stock_movements` + `inventory_damaged_movements` in one sorted stream. **Hook (`useStockMovements`):** runs both queries in parallel via `Promise.all`. Damaged rows resolve their source sub-container name via the D.11 chain (direct `source_transfer_id → warehouse_transfers.from_sub_container` preferred, fallback via `source_return_line_disposition_id → disposition.warehouse_transfer_id → transfer.from_sub_container`). Damaged rows leave `sub_container_id` null (name-only resolution) so the existing sub-container filter never accidentally cross-matches a good-stock sub with the same name. Item name/SKU pulled from the brand-variant join (damaged-movements table has no `item_name`/`sku` columns of its own). Reference type/id backfilled to `'transfer' → source_transfer_id` or `'return' → source_return_line_disposition_id` so the existing `WhMovementRefDialog` still lights up on damaged rows. Merged rows sorted by `created_at` desc, trimmed to `limit`. **Types:** `StockMovement` gains `stream: 'good' | 'damaged'`; `StockMovementType` union extended with the five `inventory_damaged_movements` types (`restock_as_damaged_in`, `send_for_repair_out`, `return_from_repair_as_writeoff`, `damaged_write_off`, `damaged_adjust`) + `damaged_return_from_repair_as_good` (already writes to `inventory_stock_movements` via Phase 9.5 but wasn't in the union). **UI (`WhMovementsTab`):** new Stream select chip (All / Good stock / Damaged stock) with `streamFilter` state wired into the same filter + page-reset effects as the other filters. Type dropdown grouped with a visual divider — "Good stock" and "Damaged stock" sections listed separately. Small red-outline "Damaged" chip renders next to the type badge on rows where `stream === 'damaged'` (both mobile card list and desktop table). **UI (`damaged-stock/page.tsx`):** Movements tab removed. Header gets a "View damaged movements" action button linking to `/master-data/warehouses?tab=movements&stream=damaged` (URL sync of query params is future work — `WhMovementsTab` reads state locally today). Dead code removed: `MovementsTab` function + `movementBadgeClass` + `movementLabel` + `MOVEMENTS_PAGE_SIZE` constant. `useDamagedMovements` hook stays exported in `useDamagedStockOverview.ts` — no callers now but a harmless dead export; killing it would need a follow-up sweep. **No flow-registry entries change** — the underlying business flows (restock as damaged, send for repair, return from repair, etc.) are unchanged; only the read-side UI moved. Operator confirmed on staging via screenshot (Stream=Damaged filter shows 6 movements with "Send for Repair" / "Restock as Damaged" type badges + red "Damaged" chip; Maintenance sub-container resolves on the Industrial Area rows via the D.11 chain, `—` on legacy Birkat rows). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md` (D.13 is an ad-hoc follow-up to D.11, not in the original plan doc).

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.11: Damaged Stock page surfaces source sub-container on all three tabs** — 2 code files (no migration). Damaged tables carry no `sub_container_id` (per D.5.a's design); the source of a damaged unit lives on the originating transfer / disposition. **Hooks:** `useDamagedOnHand` fires a second parallel query for the latest `restock_as_damaged_in` movement per `(warehouse_id, brand_variant_id)` with a `source_transfer_id → warehouse_transfers.from_sub_container_id → warehouse_sub_containers.name` join; first-wins map merged client-side. `useOutForRepair` select extended with `from_sub_container_id + to_sub_container_id + warehouse_sub_containers` name joins; `OutForRepairRow` gains `from_sub_container_id/name + to_sub_container_id/name` so the vendor's sub in the shared Repair warehouse can be surfaced alongside the source warehouse's sub. `useDamagedMovements` extended with two source-chain joins — direct `source_transfer_id → transfer.from_sub_container → name`, and disposition (`source_return_line_disposition_id → return_line_inventory_dispositions → warehouse_transfer_id → transfer.from_sub_container`). Client-side prefers the direct chain, falls back to via-disposition (covers restock rows once the vendor is assigned). All three row types gain `source_sub_container_name: string | null`. **UI (`damaged-stock/page.tsx`):** new "Source Sub-container" column on all three tabs (On-hand, Out for Repair, Movements), `hidden md:table-cell` per the plan doc's mobile-first shape. Legacy rows without a linkable source resolve to `—`. **Flow registry:** "Repair Vendor CRUD" entry updated to reflect the post-D.6.b shared model — title changed from "auto-provisioned virtual warehouse" to "sub-container of shared Repair warehouse", trigger behavior updated (BEFORE INSERT stamps `virtual_warehouse_id + sub_container_id` on the incoming row instead of creating a new warehouse), pre-D.6.b history preserved in the Downstream side-effects paragraph, D.6.b + D.6.b-cleanup migrations linked. Repair-vendor CRUD is the only registered flow whose behavior meaningfully changed from D.6.b through D.11; the read-side pageview extensions in D.7, D.9, D.10, and D.11 don't add or change flows. Operator confirmed on staging (screenshots): On-hand shows the column (both rows read `—` — staging's damaged stock originated from pre-D.4 returns before dispositions carried sub-container info); Out for Repair shows the source warehouse's sub (Birkat Alawamer Warehouse row visible); Movements shows Maintenance on the two Industrial Area rows that have D.4-era dispositions linked and `—` on the older Birkat rows. **Phase D shipped end-to-end** for the plan doc's original scope — remaining work is D.12 (parked — selective cross-division item sharing) and Phase E (deferred — drop legacy `division_id`). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.6.b cleanup: retire archived per-vendor repair warehouses via repoint-and-delete** — 1 migration (`20260801400000`). D.6.b left the pre-consolidation per-vendor warehouses as `[archived] Repair: <vendor>` rows so the 2 historical `inventory_damaged_movements.send_for_repair_out` rows referencing them stayed auditable; operator asked to hard-delete them, but `warehouse_transfers.from/to_warehouse_id` FK RESTRICT was the blocker. **Migration structure:** (1) Locate the shared Repair warehouse (RAISE EXCEPTION if D.6.b Task 1 didn't run). (2) Invariant assertion — count leaks across `fifo_cost_layers` / `inventory_stock_movements` / `warehouse_stock_allocations` / `stock_adjustments` / `receival_items` (via `receivals.warehouse_id`) / `warehouse_stock_summary` / `inventory_damaged_stock` / `inventory_damaged_stock_layers`. Any non-zero → RAISE EXCEPTION and roll back — live stock reference to an archived warehouse is a data problem, not something to sweep into the shared Repair pool. (3) Loop each `[archived]%` `is_virtual=true` warehouse: (a) UPDATE `warehouse_transfers.to_warehouse_id` + `to_sub_container_id` via `repair_vendors.sub_container_id` when `repair_vendor_id` matches; (b) UPDATE `warehouse_transfers.from_warehouse_id` + `from_sub_container_id` same way (return-from-repair direction); (c) For each old sub_container under this archived warehouse, find its vendor via any transfer header still referencing it (fallback via `warehouse_transfer_items`). Repoint `warehouse_transfer_items.sub_container_id` + any lingering `from/to_sub_container_id` on transfers. RAISE NOTICE if no vendor mapping — the DELETE will fail if anything still references it; (d) UPDATE `inventory_damaged_movements.warehouse_id` (defensive; current RPC writes `p_warehouse_id` here, but pre-D.6.b may have used the vendor's virtual warehouse); (e) DELETE the sub_containers under this warehouse; (f) DELETE the archived warehouse row. `GET DIAGNOSTICS ... ROW_COUNT` + `RAISE NOTICE` per step so the operator sees exactly what moved. (4) Idempotent — a second run finds no archived warehouses, iterates zero times, commits cleanly. **Two column-name gotchas fixed during application:** `receival_items` inherits warehouse via `receivals.warehouse_id` (not `receival_items.warehouse_id`), and the parent table is `public.receivals` (not `public.inventory_receivals`). Both first apply attempts failed cleanly with the DO block rolling back before any writes. **Applied to staging (2 archived warehouses retired):** `[archived] Repair: _test repair` → 2 to-warehouse transfers + 2 from-warehouse transfers repointed to shared Repair + vendor sub, 2 `warehouse_transfer_items` repointed, 1 sub deleted, warehouse deleted; `[archived] Repair: test 2` → no transfer history (vendor was renamed to `te3` before any use), sub had no vendor mapping but nothing referenced it so DELETE succeeded, warehouse deleted. Operator confirmed on staging: Master Data → Warehouses shows only the shared Repair card + real warehouses; damaged/repair transfer history still shows correct vendor via the sub-container. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md` (this is an ad-hoc follow-up phase to D.6.b, not in the original plan doc).

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.10: Stock Overview + Stock Value composite (warehouse, sub-container) keys + two-level tooltips + FifoDetail sub badge** — 1 migration (`20260801200500`) + 4 code files. **Schema:** `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW warehouse_stock_view` appends a new `sub_container_name` column at the end (CREATE OR REPLACE cannot reorder existing view columns — first attempt failed with `42P16 cannot change name of view column`; retried with append at tail) via `LEFT JOIN warehouse_sub_containers wsc ON wsc.id = wss.sub_container_id`. `security_invoker=true` reapplied. **Hook:** `WarehouseStockItem` type + `useWarehouseStock` select updated to carry `sub_container_name: string | null`. **UI — WhStockValueTab:** `WarehouseBreakdown` extended with `subContainerId` + `subContainerName`; merge now dedupes on the composite `(warehouseId, subContainerId)` tuple (officially undoes Task 0's warehouse-only aggregation — the temporary hotfix put in place to kill the duplicate-key React error when the summary table was expanded to include sub-containers). New `groupByWarehouse()` helper collapses the flat breakdown so Stock qty + Stock Value tooltips render a warehouse header line with total + indented sub-container rows underneath (`pl-3`, `text-[11px]`, muted color). Warehouse badge column is now a two-line stack: warehouse name on top, sub-container smaller `text-[9px] text-muted-foreground` underneath, with `flex flex-col items-start gap-0 py-1 h-auto leading-tight` keeping the badge compact. Mobile card multi-warehouse badge row shows `<warehouse> · <sub>: <qty>`. Every React `key={}` on the reshape switched to `${warehouseId}:${subContainerId ?? 'none'}`. **UI — FifoDetail (Task 3):** query fetch extended with `sub_container_id` + `warehouse_sub_containers:sub_container_id(name)` join; `FifoLayerRow` type gains `sub_container_id` + `sub_container_name`; Warehouse column renders a second muted badge next to the warehouse badge when a sub is present. **UI — WhStockOverviewTab (Task 2):** `StockTooltip` rows prop typed as `{ label, subLabel?, qty }[]`; body groups by label; if ANY row has a subLabel it renders the two-level layout (warehouse header + total, indented sub rows), otherwise falls back to the original flat rendering unchanged (preserves "Stock by Brand" tooltips). `warehouseBreakdown` useMemo emits `subLabel = item.sub_container_name`. Sub-container names render as stored — no code-side shortening; operator will rename in Master Data → Warehouses if desired (initial `shortenSubContainerName` prototype pulled back per operator ask so any naming they pick shows through verbatim). Operator confirmed on staging via screenshots: badge column two-line stack correct, hover tooltips show warehouse header + indented sub rows with correct totals, FifoDetail Warehouse column shows both badges side by side, Stock Overview tooltips render two-level for multi-sub variants. **Deferred to a follow-up phase (D.6.b cleanup):** operator wants to remove the `[archived]` old per-vendor repair warehouses left behind by D.6.b's consolidation. Blocked today by FK constraints on `warehouse_transfers.from/to_warehouse_id` and `inventory_damaged_movements.warehouse_id` — will need a repoint-and-delete migration that re-associates historical rows with the shared Repair warehouse + correct vendor `sub_container_id` before dropping the old rows. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.8: category → sub-container default routing (recursive resolver + receival pre-fill)** — 1 migration (`20260801100500`) + 7 code files. **Schema:** `ALTER TABLE inventory_categories ADD COLUMN default_sub_container_id uuid REFERENCES warehouse_sub_containers(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` (nullable, IF NOT EXISTS-guarded) + partial index on non-null values. **Resolver:** `resolve_category_sub_container(p_category_id uuid) RETURNS uuid` — STABLE SECURITY INVOKER, recursive CTE walks up `parent_id` from the given category, returns the first non-null `default_sub_container_id` (depth cap 32 as cycle guard). Grants EXECUTE to anon/authenticated/service_role. **Hooks:** `useCategorySubContainer(categoryId)` calls the resolver via `.rpc()`, hydrates the returned sub id with name + warehouse name + division_id (single follow-up select). `useBrandVariantCategory(brandVariantId)` joins `inventory_item_brand_variants → inventory_items` to fetch `category_id` for the receival dialog's lookup chain. `useInventory` — Create/Update category mutations accept `default_sub_container_id`. **UI — category admin:** `CategoryEditDialog` gained a two-select picker (warehouse first, then sub-container within that warehouse) with "Inherit from parent" as the null default. Below the picker: when nothing is set, an inline hint shows what the resolver would return by walking the parent chain (`Inherits from parent chain: <warehouse> · <sub>`). On open in edit mode with a stored `default_sub_container_id`, a one-shot query seeds the warehouse dropdown from the sub's `warehouse_id` so the pickers land on the right row. **UI — receival dialog:** `InventoryReceivalDialog` fetches the variant's category via `useBrandVariantCategory`, resolves via `useCategorySubContainer`, and after the operator picks the header warehouse, pre-fills the sub-container select IFF `resolved.warehouse_id === warehouseId` AND the resolved sub is active in that warehouse. Header warehouse always wins — no pre-fill when the resolved warehouse differs. Operator can override freely. **Types + tsc sweep:** regenerated types via `supabase gen types --linked`, re-appended the four DBTable/DBInsert/DBUpdate/AllTables helper aliases, and removed a stale `Initialising login role...` banner line that had leaked into position 1 of database.types.ts on an earlier regen. That garbage was cascading a TS1434 parse error and silencing type checking for the entire file — cleaning it up surfaced 9 pre-existing type errors in unrelated files (DivisionProvider, WhWarehousesTab, SendForRepairDialog, useRepairVendors, useSendDamagedForRepair, useWarehouseOperations, arabic-names). Those are pre-existing bugs unrelated to D.8 and will get a dedicated sweep; D.8's touched files typecheck clean. **Deferred:** D.12 (selective cross-division item sharing — `inventory_items.shared_with_division_ids uuid[]`, item edit dialog "Access & Sharing" section, cascade picker cross-division visibility, COGS to consumer division via internal transfer journal at FIFO cost, one-way sharing granted per item). Operator confirmed on staging: parent's default resolves for children, receival pre-fill fires for the matching warehouse, does not fire for a warehouse where the resolved sub doesn't live. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.6.b: consolidate repair vendors into sub-containers of one shared Repair warehouse** — 3 migrations (`20260801300000`, `20260801300100`, `20260801300200`) + 3 code files. **Schema (`20260801300000`):** `ALTER TABLE repair_vendors ADD sub_container_id NOT NULL REFERENCES warehouse_sub_containers`. Idempotent seed of ONE shared `Repair` warehouse (`is_virtual=true`, `division_id=NULL`, `location='Shared repair container — vendors as sub-containers'`). Backfill each existing vendor → create a sub-container under the shared warehouse using the current vendor name (staging had a `te3` vendor whose per-vendor warehouse still said "Repair: test 2" — sub adopts current name), redirect `virtual_warehouse_id` to the shared warehouse, archive the old per-vendor warehouse (`[archived] ` name prefix, `repair_vendor_id` cleared). Old warehouses kept so 2 historical `inventory_damaged_movements.send_for_repair_out` rows referencing them stay auditable. Trigger rewritten AFTER-INSERT → BEFORE-INSERT: stamps `NEW.virtual_warehouse_id` + `NEW.sub_container_id` on the incoming row (both NOT NULL satisfied at row-write time). **RPCs (`20260801300100`):** `rpc_send_damaged_for_repair` SELECT now fetches `sub_container_id` from `repair_vendors`; `v_to_sub_container_id := v_vendor.sub_container_id` replaces the pre-D.6.b `SELECT ... LIMIT 1 WHERE warehouse_id = v_vendor.virtual_warehouse_id` (that lookup would pick a random vendor's sub post-consolidation because every vendor's sub shares the same warehouse_id now). `rpc_return_damaged_from_repair` SELECT on `warehouse_transfers` fetches `to_sub_container_id`; `v_from_sub_container_id := v_transfer.to_sub_container_id` (send RPC stamped it with the vendor's specific sub). Legacy transfers without `to_sub_container_id` raise a hint exception rather than picking randomly. **View (`20260801300200`):** D.9 refinement discovered during D.6.b smoke — `warehouse_sub_container_totals` was `FROM fifo_cost_layers` so zero-stock sub-containers were absent. Repair vendors are zero most of the time, so the Repair card's sub-container dropdown collapsed to the plain division-label fallback. Swapped base to `FROM warehouse_sub_containers sc LEFT JOIN fifo_cost_layers`; every active sub surfaces with coalesced 0 counts. `security_invoker=false` re-applied to preserve the div-scope RLS bypass. **UI:** `useRepairVendors.ts` header comment updated + the stale "refetch afterwards" note dropped; `RepairVendorFormDialog` helper text changed from "A virtual warehouse … is auto-created" to "A sub-container is auto-created under the shared 'Repair' warehouse". Operator confirmed on staging: shared Repair card now shows both vendors in the sub-container dropdown, same UX as Birkat's Maintenance/Kitchen split. Types regenerated with `repair_vendors.sub_container_id`; helper aliases re-appended. D.6.b Task 3 (dedicated repair-vendor sub-container CRUD UI beyond `RepairVendorFormDialog`) intentionally deferred — existing dialog works end-to-end via the new trigger. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.7: sub-container column + filter across the five ledger tabs** — 6 code files. **Hook (`useWarehouseOperations`):** `StockMovement`, `WarehouseTransfer` (from + to), `StockAdjustment`, `InventoryCheck` types gain `sub_container_id` + `sub_container_name`; `ReceivalDelivery` gets `subContainerNames: string[]` (a receival's items can span multiple subs; outbound `sale_deliveries` has no header column so the array is empty — sub visible via Movements or delivery detail). Each SELECT joins `warehouse_sub_containers:sub_container_id(name)`; the transfer select adds both from + to joins. **UI:** `WhMovementsTab` gets a sub-container filter dropdown next to the warehouse filter (appears when a specific warehouse with >1 active sub is picked; resets on warehouse change) + a new "Sub-container" column at `xl:table-cell`; empty-state colSpan 10 → 11. `WhAdjustmentsTab` gains the "Sub-container" column at `xl:table-cell` (colSpan 10 → 11); local `StockAdjustmentRow` type extended; TabsList `md:overflow-x-visible` added to suppress the desktop scrollbar strip (same fix as D.5's `WhStockValueTab`). `WhTransfersTab` renders sub-container chips next to both from and to warehouse names in the Route line. `WhInventoryChecksTab` shows a sub-container chip next to `warehouse_name` in each check row. `ReceivalsDeliveriesTab` gets the "Sub-container" column at `xl:table-cell` (colSpan 8 → 9) showing single sub name for a normal receival, "N subs" for multi-sub receivals, "—" for outbound; guarded `item.subContainerNames ?? []` around length checks (same cache-served-stale-shape crash class we hit on D.9's first render). `shortenSubContainerName` used on every rendered sub name across the tabs. Operator confirmed on staging. Queue re-ordered per operator ask: **D.6.b (repair vendor consolidation into sub-containers of one Repair warehouse) bumped ahead of D.8/D.10/D.11**. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.9: sub-container breakdown + interactive filter on warehouse cards + stock tree grouping** — 2 migrations (`20260801200000`, `20260801200100`) + 6 code files. **DB:** new view `warehouse_sub_container_totals` aggregates live FIFO layers by `(warehouse_id, sub_container_id)` — `item_count = COUNT(DISTINCT brand_variant_id)` + `total_value = SUM(remaining_qty * total_unit_cost)` where `remaining_qty > 0`, matching the semantic used by the `refresh_warehouse_totals` trigger. Follow-up migration flips `security_invoker = false` on the view so it bypasses RLS on `fifo_cost_layers` — discovered mid-smoke that a Maintenance-division-scoped user saw only the Maintenance sub-container row (Kitchen filtered by `sub_container_scope_select_r → is_sub_container_visible`), which collapsed the card breakdown to a single row and the `>1` render guard skipped it. Landing-card view answers "what does this warehouse contain", so bypassing division scope is correct here. **Hook:** `useWarehouses` now runs the warehouses fetch + totals view fetch in parallel via `Promise.all`, merges results into each Warehouse row as `sub_container_breakdown: WarehouseSubContainerBreakdown[]`. **UI — Option B (interactive filter):** the "Maintenance" division badge on each card became a dashed-outline Select trigger listing every sub-container that holds live stock; "All sub-containers (N)" default. Picking a specific sub switches header totals to that sub's numbers, hides the breakdown block, filters the "Show items" tree, and carries the selection to "View in Stock Overview" via a new `initialSubContainerId` prop threaded through `handleViewStock → WhStockOverviewTab`. Warehouses with no live sub-container stock fall back to the plain division label. **UI — stock tree grouping (Task 3):** extracted `buildCategoryTree()` helper; when caller has not scoped to a specific sub AND the warehouse's stock spans multiple sub-containers, the tree renders a top-level "sub-container" band per sub — expanding a band reveals its own category tree. Sub-container name resolved via existing `useWarehouseSubContainers` + `shortenSubContainerName` helper. Single-sub warehouses and caller-scoped views (specific sub picked on the card) render the flat tree unchanged. Operator confirmed on staging: Birkat shows Maintenance (879 items / QR 1,178,492.62) + Kitchen (1 item / QR 7.50) both in breakdown block and tree grouping; filter switch flattens correctly; navigation handoff carries the sub selection. Task 0 (WhStockValueTab duplicate-key hotfix) remains in place — will be revisited in D.10 when `WarehouseBreakdown` reshape happens. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.6.a: hide virtual warehouses from operator surfaces, split card grid** — 2 files (`src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/warehouses/page.tsx`, `src/components/purchase/wh/WhWarehousesTab.tsx`). During D.6 Task 1 investigation discovered the repair model is already substantial: `repair_vendors` table + auto-provision trigger + `warehouses.is_virtual` boolean + Phase 9 RPCs (`rpc_send_for_repair`, `rpc_return_from_repair`) all live and shipped. `useWarehouses()` already defaults to `includeVirtual: false` — every operator dialog (receival / delivery / return / adjustment / transfer) was already hiding repair-vendor warehouses. The only leak was `/master-data/warehouses/page.tsx` opting in with `includeVirtual: true` and passing the same array to every child tab. Split D.6 into D.6.a (hide-only, this task, no schema/RPC change) and D.6.b (consolidate vendors into sub-containers of one Repair warehouse via trigger + RPC rewrites, separate phase). **UI:** page keeps `warehousesAll` fetch, derives `warehouses` (no virtuals) via useMemo; passes `warehouses` to `WhWarehousesTab` / `WhStockOverviewTab` / `WhStockValueTab` / `WhAdjustmentDialog` picker / `WhTransferDialog` picker, `warehousesAll` to `WhTransfersTab` / `WhAdjustmentsTab` / `WhInventoryChecksTab` / `WhMovementsTab` / `ReceivalsDeliveriesTab` (list views need historical repair activity visible). `WhWarehousesTab` refactored: split into "Main Warehouses" section (grid + stock-value comparison bar, main-only) and "Virtual Warehouses" collapsible section (defaults closed, amber wrench icon per card, tagline "Expand to see items currently sent for repair"). Operator confirmed all 5 smoke checks on staging. `is_virtual` filter was already correctly applied inside `useWarehouses` — no `kind` column added (would be redundant). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Task 0 (D.6 prep): WhStockValueTab duplicate-key hotfix** — `src/components/purchase/wh/WhStockValueTab.tsx`. Post-D.5 the stock view returns one row per (brand_variant, warehouse, sub_container). The row-merge was pushing one entry per source row into `row.warehouses`, so any warehouse with multiple sub-containers produced duplicate React keys on the badge column, "Stock by Warehouse" tooltip, and "Value by Warehouse" tooltip. Aggregate by `warehouseId` in the merge (find-or-push, sum qty/value into the existing entry) — restores the warehouse-level rollup the current UI expects. Sub-container-aware display (composite keys + two-level tooltips) lands in Phase D.10 per the D.6–D.11 plan. Operator confirmed the console error is gone. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phases-d6-d11.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.5: stock summary sub-container + return-line provenance enforcement** — 2 migrations (`20260808000100`, `20260808000200`) + 11 code files. **DB:** `warehouse_stock_summary` PK expanded to `(warehouse_id, sub_container_id, brand_variant_id)`; `refresh_stock_summary_row` gains a 3rd `p_sub_container_id` arg (old 2-arg overload dropped); `refresh_all_stock_summaries` GROUP BY adds `sub_container_id`; both triggers (`trg_fifo_refresh_stock_summary` + `trg_alloc_refresh_stock_summary`) pass NEW/OLD.sub_container_id to the helper; `warehouse_stock_view` gets DROP+CREATE to append `sub_container_id`. `refresh_all_stock_summaries()` re-runs at migration end to repopulate cleanly (1006 rows across 2 distinct sub-containers on staging). `return_lines`: row-level CHECK (`receival_item_id IS NOT NULL OR sale_delivery_line_id IS NOT NULL`) + BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger `_enforce_return_line_provenance` enforces per-source-type (PO returns require `receival_item_id`, SO returns require `sale_delivery_line_id`) — CHECK cannot cross-reference parent table's `source_type`, hence trigger. Plan-time survey returned 0 truly-orphan return_lines and 0 per-source violations, so no backfill migration was needed; Task 8 (backfill) turned into a verify-only no-op. Task 10 sale_return FIFO orphan check: 0 orphans confirmed. **UI:** `useWarehouseStock(warehouseId?, subContainerId?)` forwards the filter to the view; `useWarehouseStockByItems(bvIds, subContainerId?)` rewritten to roll sub-container rows up to per-warehouse totals when no filter is passed (preserves pre-D.5 shape for existing consumers); `useWarehouseStockSummary` sums multi-sub rows into single warehouse-level qty per variant. `queryKeys.warehouseStock` re-keyed on `(warehouseId, subContainerId)`. `WhStockOverviewTab` + `WhStockValueTab` gain a sub-container dropdown next to the warehouse select — appears when the picked warehouse has 2+ active subs, [Auto] badge with sub name when it has one, hidden when zero. New `shortenSubContainerName` helper in `useWarehouseSubContainers.ts` strips the redundant `"<Warehouse> — "` prefix from D.1 auto-created sub names so the filter row reads cleanly. `WhStockValueTab` item-type Tabs use `md:overflow-x-visible` to suppress the horizontal scroll indicator on desktop where all tabs already fit. `WhTransferDialog` drops the D.4 stopgap `.from('fifo_cost_layers')` query — scoped source stock now flows through `useWarehouseStock` with the sub-container arg; `destStockMap` aggregates sub-container rows per warehouse. `SoDeliveryDialog` / `SoDetailDialog` / `ReplacementDeliveryDialog` resolve their sub-container from (single active sub | SO.division_id match | null) and pass to `useWarehouseStockByItems` so the stock chip reflects the actual drain scope; `POReturnDetailDialog` needed no change because the hook rollup preserves existing behaviour and per-line sub-container info already surfaces via D.4.a's `ReturnLineSourceBadges`. `api/warehouse/reports/route.ts` (stock-overview + stock-value) aggregate multi-sub rows client-side into per-(warehouse, item) rows so PDF report totals match the pre-D.5 numbers; limit bumped from 5000 to 20000 to accommodate the extra fan-out. Types regenerated with `sub_container_id` on `warehouse_stock_view`; helper aliases re-appended. All 8 smoke checks confirmed by operator on staging. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phase-d5.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.4 Tasks 4–8 + D.4.c: adjustment stack, transfer allocation scope, Inv Check scoping, mobile UI** — 6 migrations (`20260807000100`–`20260807000600`) + 12 code files. **DB:** (a) `create_stock_adjustment_v2` gets optional `p_sub_container_id`; explicit pick is validated, absent value derives via `_find_or_create_sub_container(warehouse, division)`. (b) `approve_stock_adjustment_inventory` passes that id as the 5th arg to `deduct_fifo_layers` on the decrease/damage/write-off branch — the drain no longer spans sub-containers of the same warehouse. (c) Discovered mid-smoke: `warehouse_stock_allocations` NOT NULL on `sub_container_id` (from Phase C.1) tripped every transfer INSERT because `create_transfer_v2` didn't stamp the column, and the PK `(warehouse_id, brand_variant_id)` would collapse allocations across sub-containers of the same warehouse. Fix: expand PK to `(warehouse_id, brand_variant_id, sub_container_id)`, INSERT + ON CONFLICT + availability check all scoped, and `dispatch_transfer` / `cancel_transfer` / `reject_transfer_v2` allocation-decrement WHEREs filter by `from_sub_container_id`. Also stamp `sub_container_id` on the in-transit cancel/reject reversal INSERTs into `fifo_cost_layers` + `inventory_stock_movements` (both would have failed on the C.1 NOT NULL on the next in-transit cancel). (d) Hotfix: `force_approve_stock_adjustment` still referenced the pre-2026-07-24 `profiles` table (renamed to `user_data`); Owner force-approve blew up on every SA until the swap. Full sweep confirms zero remaining functions/views/policies/triggers reference the old name. (e) **D.4.c** — `inventory_checks.sub_container_id` FK added (nullable); `snapshot_inventory_check_system_qty` scopes the frozen system-qty snapshot to that sub-container's FIFO layers when set (falls back to warehouse-wide `warehouse_stock_view` for legacy checks); `apply_inventory_check_adjustments` stamps `sub_container_id` on every generated `stock_adjustments` row so the D.4 stack scopes downstream drain to the correct sub-container. Drop stale 3-arg `save_inventory_check_item_count` overload — PostgREST couldn't disambiguate the 6-arg call and the client hung on "Saving…" during the Inv Check smoke test. **UI:** `useWarehouseOperations` forwards ids on transfer/adjustment/inv-check payloads. `WhTransferDialog` renders from/to sub-container pickers with a `[Auto]` badge on single-sub warehouses, forces the item-picker Popover `side="bottom"` + caps its height at `min(480px, var(--available-height))` so the search bar stays in the viewport. `WhAdjustmentDialog` gets the same picker pattern below the warehouse select. `WhInventoryCheckStartDialog` picks a sub-container after the warehouse and filters the count list to items with FIFO qty in that sub-container. `WhInventoryCheckDetail` — hardened NaN guard on the variance calc (previously threw "Received NaN for children" when `system_qty` was null on a fresh row) and rewrote the counting UI: sub-`sm:` renders each item as a stacked card (name/brand/SKU on top, `System / Counted / Δ` strip, full-width variance-type Select) instead of the 6-column grid that was clipping past the mobile viewport. Footer bar stacks with a 44px min-h "Mark my count as complete" button. `WhInventoryChecksTab` / `WhTransfersTab` / `WhAdjustmentsTab` — headers stack on mobile so Report/New CTAs don't crush the title text. `WhItemPicker` swaps `h-[480px]` for `h-[min(480px,var(--available-height,85vh))]` so no popover ever spills past the viewport regardless of trigger position. Master Data → Warehouses page: `Stock Adjustment` button gated to `activeTab === 'adjustments'`; `Transfer Stock` gated to `activeTab === 'transfers'` — no more page-wide top-right CTAs bleeding across every tab. Types regenerated (helper aliases re-appended per `feedback_supabase_gen_types`). Operator smoke-tested Transfer golden path + multi-sub-container source, PO return scoped drain, SO return restock, Stock Adjustment (single + multi + Force Approve after the profiles→user_data swap), Inv Check start on Birkat with 2+ Maintenance sub-containers. Inv Check completion → auto-generated SA sub-container stamp still to be verified next session (deferred because the seeded check had 299 items and manual counting on mobile is impractical). Plan file: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phase-d4.md`.

- [2026-08-01] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.4 + D.4.a + D.4.b: scoped returns + restock + transfer dispatch** — 11 migrations (`20260804000100`–`20260806000800`) + 15 code files (2 new: `src/hooks/useReturnLineSources.ts`, `src/components/shared/ReturnLineSourceBadges.tsx`) + handoff doc `docs/handoff-2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-d4-d4a-d4b.md`. **D.4 Task 1:** `dispatch_transfer` now passes `warehouse_transfers.from_sub_container_id` as the 5th arg to `deduct_fifo_layers` — closes the sweep finding where transfers drained warehouse-wide. **D.4.a (PO-return provenance):** new `return_lines.receival_item_id` FK column (backfilled 2 legacy rows); `rpc_process_po_return_dispatch` derives the source sub-container per line via `return_lines → receival_items.sub_container_id` and drains scoped; PO return UI (`PoReturnsTab`, `useReceivalItemsForPo`, `usePurchaseReturns`) lists one row per receival with warehouse + sub-container badges; mutation validators hard-fail without `receival_item_id`. **D.4.b (SO-return provenance):** new `return_lines.sale_delivery_line_id` FK column (backfilled 28 legacy rows); `rpc_process_return_restock` restores per-line to the source sub-container derived from `sale_delivery_line_id → sale_deliveries → cogs_entries.source_id → fifo_cost_layers.sub_container_id` (Task 5 subsumes the original D.4 Task 3); `rpc_create_partial_replacement`'s write_off branch stamps `sub_container_id` using the same chain; `rpc_complete_return_inspection` carries `sale_delivery_line_id` + `receival_item_id` from source line to split rows, and `p_restock_warehouse_id` becomes `DEFAULT NULL` and informational only; SO return UI (`CreateReturnDialog`, `CompleteInspectionDialog`, `SaleReturnDetailDialog`, `useSaleReturns`, `useSaleDeliveryLinesForSo`) reworked to pick delivery-line rows and drop the header-level Restock Warehouse dropdown. Replacement dialog keeps operator-choice source with `[Auto]` default + `Change` affordance (replacement stock can come from any warehouse). Fallback chain for legacy data: exact FIFO-layer derive → `_find_or_create_sub_container(warehouse, division)` for pre-D.3 deliveries → division cascade (return → SO → warehouse) → hard-fail with operator-facing hint. New shared `<ReturnLineSourceBadges>` + `useReturnLineSources(receivalIds, deliveryLineIds, returnId?)` renders provenance badges (warehouse + sub-container) on expanded return rows with a single batched query per view. Types regenerated + helper aliases re-appended. User smoke-tested all 7 checks on staging. Handoff doc lists the 4 known limitations (no DB NOT NULL yet, existing legacy movements not re-stamped, `sale_deliveries.warehouse_id` is ledger truth, replacement FIFO drain not scoped in this phase). Plans: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-01-warehouse-model-v2-phase-d4.md`, `..._d4a-po-return-provenance.md`, `..._d4b-sale-return-provenance.md`.

- [2026-07-31] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.3: Sale delivery scoped to sub-container** — 8 files (4 migrations + 4 UI/hooks). **RPCs** — `deduct_fifo_layers` (migration `20260803001800`) gains optional `p_sub_container_id`; when null, backward-compat for the ~7 other callers, when set, restricts the FIFO SELECT to layers within that sub-container. `complete_delivery_inventory` (`20260803001900`) — 3-arg (drops the 2-arg overload) — resolves the target sub-container from (warehouse × `sale_orders.division_id`) or accepts explicit `p_sub_container_id` (validated against the delivery's warehouse + SO's division). Passes it to `deduct_fifo_layers`, stamps `inventory_stock_movements.sub_container_id`, adds `cogs_entries.source_id` FK to capture the drained layer for exact-cancel restore. `cancel_delivery_inventory` restores each drained layer to the SAME sub-container it came from via `cogs_entries.source_id → fifo_cost_layers.sub_container_id`, with a fallback to `_find_or_create_sub_container` if the layer was purged. Two hotfixes on the deduct rewrite: `20260803002000` aliases `fifo_cost_layers` as `fcl.` so the OUT parameters `source_type` / `source_id` / `sub_container_id` don't clash with the SELECT's unqualified column refs (same class of regression as Section-10 hotfix `20260727080000`, reintroduced by C.2.d); `20260803002100` swaps a stale `inventory_brand_variants` reference for the post-rename `inventory_item_brand_variants`. **UI** — `DeliveryFormDialog.tsx` fetches active sub-containers for the picked warehouse via `useWarehouseSubContainers`, filters by SO.division_id when set, otherwise offers every active sub-container. Renders a compact "Sub-container: `<name>` [Auto]" badge / picker / red empty-state below the warehouse select. Submit gated on a resolvable id and forwards it as `subContainerId`. `SoDetailDialog.tsx` — "Delivered" button on a pending delivery routes to the full `DeliveryFormDialog` when the SO has no `division_id` set (legacy or replacement flows against pre-division-switcher SOs), so the operator can pick a sub-container; when division is set, keeps the existing `AlertDialog` shortcut (RPC auto-derives). `useSaleDeliveries.useCompleteDelivery` — payload gains `subContainerId?: string | null`, forwarded as `p_sub_container_id`. `useSaleOrders` — `SaleOrder` type gains `division_id`, `SaleDelivery` gains `source_credit_note_id` (drifted vs. DB). Operator confirmed the golden path works end-to-end on staging: DEL-00030 (SO-00017, no division) opened the picker, selected Birkat Alawamer — Maintenance — Maintenance, submitted, delivered cleanly. Closes a pre-existing scope bug where a Kitchen delivery could drain Maintenance stock in a shared warehouse. Codex sweep flagged D.4 scope gaps (`dispatch_transfer`, `rpc_process_po_return_dispatch`, `rpc_process_return_restock`, adjustment stack) — folded into upcoming phases. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-31-warehouse-model-v2-phase-d3.md`.

- [2026-07-31] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase D.2: Receival forms surface sub-container** — 10 files (6 code + 4 migrations, ~950 lines added). Adds required `p_sub_container_id`, validates active + warehouse-owned, populates 5 stock-side INSERTs. - [2026-07-31] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase C.3: `is_sub_container_visible()` + additive RLS** — `supabase/migrations/20260803001100_warehouse_model_v2_phase_c3.sql` (207 lines). Existing `division_scope_r` policies from `20260731000000` remain in place — both layers must pass through Phase D; Phase E drops the legacy layer. Applied cleanly to staging `mwvblpgbgxipvrevkeff`. No RPC / UI changes in this phase — reads and writes still work identically because every row already carries a valid `sub_container_id` (Phase C.2.f enforced NOT NULL) and every stock-writing RPC now populates it (Phase C.2.b–e).

- [2026-07-31] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase C.2 complete (b/c/d/e/f)** — 5 migrations `20260803000600` through `_001000`. Every stock-writing RPC on staging now populates `sub_container_id` via the `_find_or_create_sub_container` helper from C.2.a; the 8 sub_container_id FK columns across the 6 stock tables + both `warehouse_transfers` FKs are now NOT NULL. Breakdown: **C.2.b (`20260803000600`, 331 lines)** rewrote `create_and_approve_receival` (division from `purchase_orders.division_id`) + `rpc_process_return_restock` (division from `so_po_returns.division_id`); populates sub_container_id on `receival_items`, `fifo_cost_layers`, `inventory_stock_movements`. **C.2.c (`20260803000700`, 449 lines)** rewrote `create_transfer_v2` (DROP + CREATE with 2 new optional params `p_from_sub_container_id`, `p_to_sub_container_id`; backward-compat resolves oldest active sub-container per warehouse; aborts if multi-division warehouse has >1 to prevent silent misplacement — deliberately does NOT auto-create), `dispatch_transfer`, `receive_transfer`; source items flip to destination sub-container on receive. **C.2.d (`20260803000800`, 245 lines)** rewrote `deduct_fifo_layers` (added `sub_container_id` to RETURNS TABLE, sourced from drained layer) + `approve_stock_adjustment_inventory` (design doc's "apply_adjustment" is actually named this; legacy `apply_adjustment(uuid)` targets dead `inventory_adjustments` table and stays untouched). **C.2.e (`20260803000900`, 428 lines)** rewrote `rpc_send_damaged_for_repair` + `rpc_return_damaged_from_repair` (both populate from + to sub_container FKs on the outbound + inbound warehouse_transfers headers, items + fifo_cost_layers + inventory_stock_movements). `_record_inventory_disposition` NOT touched — its stock-side INSERTs land in `inventory_damaged_stock_layers` / `inventory_damaged_stock` / `inventory_damaged_movements`, none of which have `sub_container_id` (Phase A didn't add the column to those). **C.2.f (`20260803001000`, 109 lines)** belt-and-braces backfill (re-ran Phase B logic to catch any rows written between Phase A schema land and each C.2.x rewrite), assertion (aborts on any NULL remaining), then 8 SET NOT NULL flips. All migrations applied cleanly to staging `mwvblpgbgxipvrevkeff`. Every rewrite sourced live function bodies via reverse-chron grep across migrations (per `feedback_rewrite_functions_from_live_db`). One design deviation from the spec: 3 of the 6 "stock tables" (`warehouse_stock_allocations`, `stock_adjustments`, `warehouse_transfer_items`) have no `division_id` column — trigger `_sync_division_from_sub_container` only attached to the 3 that do (`fifo_cost_layers`, `inventory_stock_movements`, `receival_items`); the other 3 will be gated via `sub_container_id` alone in Phase C.3.

- [2026-07-31] **Warehouse Model v2 — Phase C.2.a: sub-container helper + division-sync triggers** — `supabase/migrations/20260803000500_warehouse_model_v2_phase_c2a.sql`. New helper `public._find_or_create_sub_container(warehouse_id, division_id)` returns the sub-container id for the pair, auto-creating one on first call (used by C.2.b–C.2.e RPC rewrites). Uses `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` for NULL-equal comparison so virtual-warehouse sub-containers match. New shared trigger `_sync_division_from_sub_container` BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE of `sub_container_id` — always overrides `NEW.division_id` from the sub-container so the two denormalized copies can never drift; rows with NULL `sub_container_id` (pre-C.2.b writes) keep whatever `division_id` was explicitly passed. Trigger attached to the 3 stock tables that have a `division_id` column: `fifo_cost_layers`, `inventory_stock_movements`, `receival_items`. Pre-flight grep revealed only 3 of the 6 stock tables have `division_id` (design doc assumed all 6); the other 3 (`warehouse_stock_allocations`, `stock_adjustments`, `warehouse_transfer_items`) will gate via `sub_container_id` only in Phase C.3. Pure infrastructure — nothing behaves differently yet.
