Three hotfixes shipped in the same task during operator testing (see next three rows).

- [2026-07-31] **Hotfix: damaged-stock RPCs stored auth.uid() into columns that FK to user_data(id)** — `supabase/migrations/20260802000660_fix_damaged_rpcs_user_data_lookup.sql`. Same class of bug as `20260729220554_fx_gain_loss_map_auth_uid_to_user_data.sql` (FX module) and `20260802000360_repair_vendor_trigger_security_definer.sql` (9.2). Operator submitting a restock_as_damaged / send_for_repair disposition through the ReplacementDeliveryDialog UI hit `ERROR: insert or update on table "inventory_damaged_stock_layers" violates foreign key constraint "..._created_by_fkey"`. Root cause: in this project `auth.uid()` (auth users id) and `user_data.id` (profile primary key) are distinct — `user_data.auth_user_id` is the FK linking them. Storing raw `auth.uid()` into a column that FKs `user_data(id)` fails at insert for every real caller. Manual SQL-editor tests missed it because the postgres role's `auth.uid()` returns NULL and the FK columns are nullable — NULL was accepted. Only real authenticated UI calls surfaced the bug. **Affected columns:** `inventory_damaged_stock_layers.created_by`, `inventory_damaged_movements.created_by`, `warehouse_transfers.created_by_profile_id / dispatched_by_profile_id / received_by_profile_id` (all FK user_data(id) since the 2026-07-24 profiles→user_data rename). **Fix:** introduced shared helper `public._current_user_data_id()` that resolves user_data.id from auth.uid() (LIMIT 1), returns NULL when no match (nullable FK still accepts NULL — service-role paths still work). All three RPCs (`_record_inventory_disposition`, `rpc_send_damaged_for_repair`, `rpc_return_damaged_from_repair`) rewritten via CREATE OR REPLACE to compute `v_uid := _current_user_data_id()` once at the top and use v_uid at every FK-to-user_data insert site. Other auth.uid() call sites where the target column is plain uuid with no FK (`return_line_inventory_dispositions.created_by`) are left unchanged. Rule going forward: never store `auth.uid()` directly into a column that FKs `user_data(id)` — use `_current_user_data_id()` instead.

- [2026-07-31] **Hotfix: credit_notes + debit_notes RLS lockout for return-based rows** — `supabase/migrations/20260802000650_fix_credit_debit_notes_rls_return_based.sql`. Operator reported `/sales/credit-notes` empty even with "All Divisions" active — 21/21 CNs invisible. Root cause: the Division Switcher RLS backfill (`20260731000000_rls_division_scope_backfill.sql:187-197`) added RESTRICTIVE policies that checked ONLY the `invoice_id → so_invoices.division_id` path for credit_notes (`bill_id → bills.division_id` for debit_notes). Return-based notes have `invoice_id`/`bill_id = NULL` because the customer/supplier was never invoiced separately — the CN/DN is booked directly against the return. Single EXISTS clause on NULL FK returns no rows → RESTRICTIVE denies → row invisible for everyone including users `is_division_visible()` would otherwise allow. Confirmed via `select count(*) filter (where invoice_id is null and source_return_id is not null) from credit_notes; → (21, 21)` — 100% return-based. **Fix:** rewrote all 8 policies (4 per table × 2 tables) as a three-branch resolver — invoice/bill parent primary, `source_return_id → so_po_returns.division_id` fallback when the primary FK is NULL, defensive owner/accountant-only path for the (should-never-happen) neither-parent case. **Full RLS audit** of the backfill migration also completed to confirm this was the only lockout: all other child-via-EXISTS policies (11 tables) target parent FKs that are NOT NULL in schema; payments + payment_plans multi-parent resolvers already have IS NOT NULL guards; direct-gated tables (own division_id) are unaffected because `is_division_visible(NULL)` returns TRUE. Separately noted low-severity leak (not fixed here): 9.4's `rpc_send_damaged_for_repair` doesn't set `warehouse_transfers.division_id`, so damaged_repair_out transfers land with NULL division and become globally visible — not sensitive but inconsistent; flagged for 9.7 cleanup.

- [2026-07-31] **Hotfix: filter virtual repair warehouses from operator pickers** — `src/hooks/useWarehouses.ts`, `src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/warehouses/page.tsx`. After 9.2 shipped auto-provisioned virtual warehouses per repair vendor (name `Repair: <vendor>`), those rows started appearing in every operator warehouse picker — Restock warehouse dropdown on returns, Create PO warehouse, receival source, delivery source, transfer picker. Screenshot from operator confirmed. Virtual warehouses are internal-only transfer targets; must never be operator-selectable. **Fix:** `useWarehouses({ includeVirtual?: boolean })` — default `false` adds `.or('is_virtual.is.null,is_virtual.eq.false')` to the query. Query cache key includes the option so both variants coexist. All existing callers pass no argument → default false → virtual filtered out. Only Master Data → Warehouses page opts in with `{ includeVirtual: true }` so admins can still see/manage the auto-provisioned rows.

- [2026-07-30] **Phase 9 Sub-task 9.5: rpc_return_damaged_from_repair (good/writeoff/mixed outcomes)** — 2 migrations (`supabase/migrations/20260802000600_rpc_return_from_repair.sql` + hotfix `_000610_fix_rpc_return_from_repair_items_column.sql`), `src/types/database.types.ts`, `docs/flows-registry.md`. Closes the send-for-repair loop opened by 9.4. New `stock_movement_type` enum value `damaged_return_from_repair_as_good` (Postgres 15 allows ADD VALUE inside a txn since our RPC body only casts it at CALL time, not migration time). New RPC `rpc_return_damaged_from_repair(p_transfer_id, p_outcome, p_qty_good, p_qty_writeoff, p_repair_cost=0, p_notes=null)` — `p_outcome ∈ {good, writeoff, mixed}` is a sanity check on the qtys (raises if the pick disagrees). Good branch: fresh `fifo_cost_layers` row at source warehouse (source_type='damaged_repair_return', source_id=outbound transfer id) with `unit_cost = original_base + (p_repair_cost / p_qty_good)` — repair cost amortized across good units, writeoff units are sunk expense; `inventory_item_brand_variants.stock_level` bump; `inventory_stock_movements` row with the new enum value; `recalc_average_cost` weighted-avg recompute; new inbound `warehouse_transfers` row (kind=damaged_repair_return_good, status=received, repair_cost stamped); normalized `warehouse_transfer_items` child row. Writeoff branch: `inventory_damaged_movements` row of type `return_from_repair_as_writeoff` (unit_cost = original, no repair-cost inheritance); inbound transfer row (kind=damaged_repair_return_writeoff) with a child item row for symmetric join reads (received_qty=0). Outbound close: status=received, received_at=now(), received_by=auth.uid(), repair_cost stamped. **Two intentional departures from the plan doc:** (1) plan linked inbound-to-outbound via a JSON string in `notes`; rejected in favor of the existing `warehouse_transfers.source_return_line_disposition_id` column (permitted by the 9.2 CHECK for return kinds) so JOINs replace text-JSON parsing; (2) plan assumed `_add_good_stock_layer` helper; that helper does not exist, so the good-stock addition is inlined using the receival/adjustment pattern. **Hotfix in same task:** initial 9.5 SELECTed `warehouse_transfers.items` inside the FOR UPDATE lock — that jsonb column was DROPPED by `20260715140000_drop_warehouse_transfers_items_json.sql` back in July. Root cause: I read the shape from stale `baseline_schema.sql` (which still shows the pre-normalization body) and didn't check the follow-up migrations. 9.4's `rpc_send_damaged_for_repair` correctly used only normalized child rows; 9.5 should have inherited that shape from the sibling function it composes with. Patch `_000610` restored the correct body via CREATE OR REPLACE; source-migration `_000600` also updated in place so fresh clones apply the correct body from the start. Verified end-to-end on staging: transfer `d44c39dd` (the 9.4-created outbound) returned as outcome=good/qty=1/repair=10 → outbound status=received/repair_cost=10, new inbound transfer damaged_repair_return_good/received/repair_cost=10, new fifo layer qty=1/unit_cost=10 (0 base + 10 repair / 1 unit), new movement damaged_return_from_repair_as_good qty=1/unit_cost=10.

- [2026-07-30] **Phase 9 Sub-task 9.4: send_for_repair disposition RPC + rpc_send_damaged_for_repair** — `supabase/migrations/20260802000500_rpc_send_for_repair.sql`, `src/types/database.types.ts`, `docs/flows-registry.md`. Last "not yet implemented" disposition stub from Phase 7/8 gone. Constraint relax on `return_line_inventory_dispositions_link_matches_type` allows `send_for_repair` rows to exist with `warehouse_transfer_id` null (needed for the two-step UX — disposition first, vendor pick later). New helper `_consume_damaged_stock_fifo(warehouse_id, brand_variant_id, qty)` walks `inventory_damaged_stock_layers` FIFO and updates the aggregate. New RPC `rpc_send_damaged_for_repair(disposition_id, vendor_id, warehouse_id, expected_return_date, notes)`: (a) validates disposition state + vendor active + vendor has virtual warehouse + source ≠ vendor virtual warehouse, (b) implicit restock-into-damaged if aggregate at (warehouse, variant) is short of the qty (mirrors 9.3 restock leg exactly — same FIFO layer + weighted-avg upsert + `restock_as_damaged_in` movement, attributed to THIS disposition), (c) creates `warehouse_transfers` header with `transfer_kind='damaged_repair_out'` + one normalized `warehouse_transfer_items` child row (matches `create_transfer_v2` pattern), (d) `_consume_damaged_stock_fifo` decrements source-warehouse damaged stock, (e) emits `send_for_repair_out` movement linked to both disposition + transfer, (f) links `return_line_inventory_dispositions.warehouse_transfer_id` to the new transfer. Both dispatchers (`rpc_record_inventory_disposition` + `rpc_create_partial_replacement`) now dispatch `send_for_repair` through `_record_inventory_disposition`; the recorder gains a guard requiring `p_warehouse_id` for `send_for_repair` (catches misconfigured callers at disposition-creation time). **Corrections vs plan:** plan referenced `_next_transfer_number('SFR')` — real helper is `generate_transfer_number()` with no arg (produces `WT-YYYY-NNNNN`); plan wrote only to `warehouse_transfers.items` jsonb — real pattern uses BOTH header + normalized child rows (mirrors `create_transfer_v2`). Verified end-to-end on staging: disposition `b9c44927-2ffd-4cb5-b98a-d880e56fbedf` created send_for_repair on SR return `a82d9b2d`, follow-up RPC produced transfer `d44c39dd-fd9a-411e-98b4-002ec147f94b` targeting `_test repair` vendor with virtual warehouse `ebb4997d`; both movement rows (restock_in + send_out) present, damaged stock net zero at source. Task included two unrelated hotfixes discovered during operator testing (see next two rows).

- [2026-07-30] **Hotfix: PoPaymentDialog Due used a QAR round-trip that inflated the balance** — `src/components/purchase/PoPaymentDialog.tsx`. On PO-2026-07-015 (USD, booked 3.65) with an existing $24,150 payment made at rate 3.62, the Record Payment dialog showed Paid USD 23,951.507 and Due USD 24,348.493 — off by $198.493 from the correct USD 24,150 / USD 24,150. The dialog summed `payments.amount_qar` (87,423 at rate 3.62) and divided by the PO's booked rate (3.65), which implicitly assumed all payments were made at the booked rate — but each payment carries its own exchange rate. Fix: sum `payments.amount` (already in PO currency) directly. Brings PO side into sync with `SoPaymentDialog.tsx:37` which never had this bug. Reported by Mohamed Ismail Mohideen with a detailed teardown of the underlying accounting logic (the FX inventory-valuation semantics he raised alongside the bug were considered but discarded — Model A / booked-rate stays; documented for future reference should the accounting model ever need to change).

- [2026-07-30] **Hotfix: infinite recursion in payments FX AFTER trigger** — `supabase/migrations/20260802000550_fix_fx_recompute_recursion.sql`. "stack depth limit exceeded" when recording a payment on a foreign-currency PO/SO that already had ≥1 payment (exponential fan-out per existing row). Root cause chain: BEFORE trigger `_trg_payments_compute_fx` correctly sets `NEW.exchange_gain/loss` → AFTER trigger `_trg_payments_refresh_document_fx` calls `rpc_recompute_document_fx(v_type, v_id)` → RPC issues `UPDATE payments SET exchange_rate = exchange_rate` (intentional — re-fires BEFORE trigger for stale existing rows so they recompute) → but that UPDATE ALSO re-fires the AFTER trigger for every row it touched → each nested firing calls `rpc_recompute_document_fx` again → each of those UPDATE-all-payments again → recursion. Fix: transaction-local guard flag `mms.fx_recompute_active`. RPC sets it to '1' before its self-touch UPDATE and clears immediately after; AFTER trigger short-circuits when the flag is set. Uses `set_config(..., is_local=true)` so COMMIT/ROLLBACK/error auto-resets — no cleanup path needed. Rejected `pg_trigger_depth()` alternative because it wouldn't cover `rpc_update_document_initial_rate` calling `rpc_recompute_document_fx` directly (not via trigger, so depth=0 and the AFTER cascade would still fire). Bug hid during FX module verification because that test used a PO with only one payment — the recursion fires but doesn't have any existing rows to multiply through. First multi-payment foreign PO in staging exposed it. Verified: payment on PO-2026-07-015 now saves cleanly.

- [2026-07-30] **Phase 9 Sub-task 9.3: restock_as_damaged disposition RPC** — 2 migrations (`20260802000400_rpc_restock_as_damaged.sql`, `_000410_fix_rpc_partial_replacement_regression.sql`), `src/types/database.types.ts`, `docs/flows-registry.md`. First of two disposition stubs from Phase 7/8 now real. Replaces `raise exception 'restock_as_damaged is not yet implemented'` in both `_record_inventory_disposition` and `rpc_create_partial_replacement` (the dispatch loop) with the A2 damaged-side write flow: guard the return line is `condition='damaged'` and qty ≤ `inventory_remaining_qty`, insert the disposition row (both FKs null), append a FIFO layer into `inventory_damaged_stock_layers` at `p_warehouse_id`, upsert `inventory_damaged_stock` with weighted-average unit cost, and stamp the ledger via `inventory_damaged_movements(movement_type='restock_as_damaged_in')` linked back to the disposition id. Unit cost sourced from the existing `_return_line_fifo_unit_cost(p_return_id, p_return_line_id, p_qty)` helper — `return_lines` has no `unit_price`/`unit_cost` column (Phase 8.7 confirmed). **Signature change** on `_record_inventory_disposition` — trailing `p_warehouse_id uuid default null` added; the restock_as_damaged branch raises if null, other branches ignore. **Constraint change**: dropped+re-added `return_line_inventory_dispositions_link_matches_type` — restock_as_damaged rows now permit both FKs null (aligns with A2 — no `inventory_stock_movements` row is written for this disposition, only the damaged-side ledger). `send_for_repair` still requires `warehouse_transfer_id NOT NULL` — Task 9.4 will relax that. **One regression caught by task-review + fixed inline** (`_000410`): the initial 9.3 subagent rebuilt `rpc_create_partial_replacement` from the stale `20260730000500` body (broken columns `delivery_date`/`division_id`/`notes` on `sale_deliveries`, `rl.unit_price` in SELECT, `qty`/`unit_price`/`is_gift` on `sale_delivery_lines`) instead of the current-live `20260731000700` shape (correct: `date`/`return_id`, `qty_delivered` only). Patch migration restored the correct body with only the restock_as_damaged branch layered on top. Two manual-test dispositions left on staging (SR-00002 line `92382de9…`, SR-00014 line `b552bce5…` — each 1 unit) — kept intentionally as real end-to-end proof; product owner accepted the drift as staging-appropriate.

- [2026-07-30] **Phase 9 Sub-task 9.2: repair vendors + virtual warehouses + damaged transfer_kind** — 4 migrations (`20260802000200_repair_vendors.sql`, `_000300_damaged_transfers.sql`, `_000350_warehouses_division_virtual_fix.sql`, `_000360_repair_vendor_trigger_security_definer.sql`), `src/types/database.types.ts`, `docs/flows-registry.md`. New `repair_vendors` table (deliberately separate from `suppliers` — repair shops aren't goods vendors, product decision from 2026-07-30 brainstorm) with an AFTER-INSERT trigger `_repair_vendor_provision_warehouse()` (SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public) that auto-creates a `warehouses` row named `Repair: <vendor>` with `is_virtual=true, repair_vendor_id=NEW.id, division_id=NULL` and back-links `repair_vendors.virtual_warehouse_id`. Virtual repair warehouses are shared across divisions — product decision confirmed during execution. Extended `warehouse_transfers` with a `transfer_kind` discriminator (`good_stock | damaged_repair_out | damaged_repair_return_good | damaged_repair_return_writeoff`) + 4 supporting columns (`repair_vendor_id`, `source_return_line_disposition_id`, `expected_return_date`, `repair_cost`) + a shape CHECK that enforces the right combination for each kind; two indexes on `transfer_kind` and `repair_vendor_id`. **Two runtime fixes discovered during subagent execution:** (1) `warehouses.division_id` was NOT NULL (from the Division Switcher migration) which broke the auto-provision on every repair_vendors insert — fix `_000350` dropped the blanket NOT NULL and replaced with a conditional CHECK `is_virtual OR division_id IS NOT NULL`; real warehouses still require a division, virtual ones may be null. (2) Trigger function was created without `security definer` / `set search_path = public` — task reviewer flagged as Critical because the trigger writes to `warehouses` (RLS-enabled) on behalf of an authenticated user whose INSERT policy may reject virtual (null division_id) rows; patch `_000360` recreated the function with the security clauses. Flow registered in `flows-registry.md` as "Repair Vendor CRUD (auto-provisioned virtual warehouse)" — Active status since the DB-level flow is live, UI ships in Phase 9.6. Applied to staging (mwvblpgbgxipvrevkeff); type regen included the new tables + columns with helper aliases re-appended.

- [2026-07-30] **Phase 9 Sub-task 9.1: damaged stock schema (A2 — dedicated tables)** — `supabase/migrations/20260802000100_damaged_stock_tables.sql`, `src/types/database.types.ts`. Three new tables land the foundation for `restock_as_damaged` + `send_for_repair` dispositions: `inventory_damaged_stock (warehouse_id, brand_variant_id, qty, weighted_unit_cost, updated_at)` with composite PK — parallel to `inventory_stock` but keeps the blast radius zero on all existing sale/delivery/picker queries (Decision A2, chosen over A1's condition-flag approach during the 2026-07-30 brainstorm); `inventory_damaged_stock_layers` for FIFO layer tracking per receipt into damaged stock; `inventory_damaged_movements` with a 5-value text CHECK (`restock_as_damaged_in`, `send_for_repair_out`, `return_from_repair_as_writeoff`, `damaged_write_off`, `damaged_adjust`) — deliberately separate from the `inventory_stock_movement_type` postgres enum since damaged movements have their own axis. RLS enabled on all three, one authenticated SELECT policy each (writes route through SECURITY DEFINER RPCs in 9.3–9.5). Indexes on `(warehouse_id, brand_variant_id, layered_at)` for FIFO consumption, `(warehouse_id, brand_variant_id, created_at)` for movement history, and both source FKs (`source_return_line_disposition_id`, `source_transfer_id`) for the audit trail. **Sub-task 9.0** (staging baseline marker) collapsed into verify-only — `db push --dry-run` already reported `Remote database is up to date.` and the `20260801xxxxxx` timestamp range was fully occupied by division-switcher / hook-fix / brand-rename migrations, so the marker file was dropped and Phase 9's migration base shifted to `20260802xxxxxx`. **Two schema-drift catches surfaced during 9.1 execution** — both saved as memory in `reference_table_renames.md`: (1) `public.brand_variants` referenced in the plan doesn't exist — renamed to `inventory_item_brand_variants` on 2026-07-24, compat view later dropped; (2) `public.profiles` renamed to `user_data` in the same rename cycle. Migration corrected to `inventory_item_brand_variants` and `user_data` in all 5 FK references. Task-review clean (spec ✅, quality ✅, zero findings). Applied to staging (mwvblpgbgxipvrevkeff); dev catch-up deferred to post-Phase-9 sub-task.

- [2026-07-30] **Division Switcher PR #2: active-division picker + server-enforced narrowing** — 4 staging migrations (`20260801000000_division_switcher_active_division.sql`, `_000100_grant_auth_hook_exec.sql`, `_000200_fix_hook_no_max_uuid.sql`); new files `src/components/providers/DivisionProvider.tsx`, `src/components/layout/DivisionSwitcher.tsx`, `src/components/layout/DivisionMismatchChip.tsx`; modified `src/app/(dashboard)/layout.tsx` (provider mount), `src/components/layout/UserMenu.tsx` (menu item), `src/components/master-data/WarehouseFormDialog.tsx` (active-default), `src/app/(dashboard)/purchase/create-po/page.tsx` + `sales/create-so/page.tsx` (active-default), `src/app/(dashboard)/purchase/edit-po/[id]/page.tsx` + `sales/edit-so/[id]/page.tsx` (mismatch chip), `src/app/(dashboard)/purchase/orders/page.tsx` + `sales/orders/page.tsx` (removed `<DivisionFilter>`); deleted `src/components/shared/DivisionFilter.tsx` + `src/components/layout/DivisionFilter.tsx`; stripped `divisionId`/`divisionIds` filter params from `usePurchaseOrders.ts` + `useSaleOrders.ts`. DB: adds `user_data.active_division_id` column, extends `is_division_visible()` to narrow to the JWT `active_division_id` claim when present, updates `custom_access_token_hook` to inject the claim, adds `set_active_division(uuid)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC that validates target is in caller's access set before writing, adds safety trigger on `user_company_divisions` DELETE to null active_division_id if a user loses access. Client: `<DivisionProvider>` mounted in dashboard layout reads active from JWT + localStorage; `useActiveDivision()` exposes `setActiveDivision(id | null)` which optimistically updates → RPC → `supabase.auth.refreshSession()` (new JWT with new claim) → `queryClient.invalidateQueries()` (all list caches refetch, RLS filters server-side). `DivisionSwitcherMenuItem` renders inline inside the profile dropdown with native-radio-style visuals (custom `RadioDot` — hollow circle with inner dot when selected) — "DIVISION" heading, "All Divisions" for super-viewers, one radio per accessible division; `closeOnClick={false}` keeps the menu open after picking. `DivisionMismatchChip` shows a subtle amber "Viewing X · not your active" badge on edit-po/edit-so headers when the record's division ≠ caller's active. Create-page defaults: create-po/create-so/WarehouseFormDialog default their local `divisionId` to active-division (falls back to sole-division if only one accessible). Three sub-fixes landed during verification: (1) `20260801000100_grant_auth_hook_exec.sql` — Supabase Auth calls hooks as `supabase_auth_admin`; needed explicit GRANT EXECUTE (previously threw "Error running hook URI" on every login attempt); (2) `20260801000200_fix_hook_no_max_uuid.sql` — my original hook body used `MAX(uuid)` to pass through active_division_id, but Postgres has no `max(uuid)` aggregator — hook threw at every token issuance until split into two queries; (3) rewrote `DivisionSwitcherMenuItem` twice — first attempt used `DropdownMenuSub`/`DropdownMenuRadioItem` combo but the shadcn/base-ui `MenuItem` uses `onClick` not `onSelect` so radio clicks did nothing; and the default `RadioItemIndicator` renders a check not a radio dot. Final version uses `DropdownMenuItem onClick={onSelect} closeOnClick={false}` + custom `RadioDot`. **Operational discovery:** staging Supabase project had no auth hooks registered at all — enabling the "Customize Access Token (JWT) Claims hook" in dashboard → Authentication → Hooks and pointing it at `public.custom_access_token_hook` was a manual step, not a migration. Users must sign out + sign in once to pick up the new JWT with `user_type` / `division_ids` / `active_division_id` claims. **Wrong-DB discovery:** PR #1 (RLS backfill) + this PR's migrations were originally pushed to production project `wkmvjxxmzstsvahuiwsz` because `supabase/config.toml` pointed there; this session relinked to staging `mwvblpgbgxipvrevkeff` and re-pushed all three migrations. Prod-side reversal tracked as follow-up. Spec doc: `docs/division-switcher-design.md`. Combined effort with PR #1 was ~1 working day of implementation + ~3h of environmental debugging.
- [2026-07-30] **Division Switcher PR #1: RLS division-scope backfill** — `supabase/migrations/20260731000000_rls_division_scope_backfill.sql`, `docs/division-switcher-design.md` — 104 RESTRICTIVE policies across 26 tables covering SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE via existing `is_division_visible(uuid)` helper. **11 directly-gated tables** (bills, cogs_entries, fifo_cost_layers, inventory_stock_movements, po_approval_chains, receival_items, receivals, so_invoices, so_po_returns, warehouse_transfers, warehouses) use `is_division_visible(division_id)` directly. **15 child tables via EXISTS-subquery on parent** (bill_line_items, invoice_line_items, po_line_items, sale_order_lines, return_lines, payments, payment_plans, payment_bill_allocations, credit_notes, debit_notes, sale_deliveries, stock_adjustments, warehouse_transfer_items, shipments, warehouse_stock_allocations). Payments policy has a 4-branch resolver (bill_id / invoice_id / source_type='purchase_order' / source_type='sale_order') covering all valid parent shapes. RESTRICTIVE choice means these AND with existing permissive `USING(true)` rules — no destructive drops, so role-based rules like the `so_invoices` void check stay intact. Pre-migration only `purchase_orders`/`sale_orders` had server-side division enforcement; UI-side filter was the only gate on everything else — meaning a Maintenance-division user could in principle read Cleaning-division invoices/inventory/POs via direct API call. Deferred (workflow/config-only, no money leak): po_approval_chain_tiers, po_approvals, po_edit_requests, po_rfq_quotes, po_versions, receival_edit_requests, inventory_checks, warehouse_reorder_points, warehouse_responsible_persons, tool_asset_units, return_line_inventory_dispositions. Sweep of every direct `.insert/.update` call on the 26 gated tables verified INSERTs won't fail RLS: `useSaleOrders:858` + `useSaleDeliveries:138` insert sale_deliveries via parent SO that must be visible; `useSaleOrders:939` + `useCreditNotes:331` + `usePurchaseOrders:740` insert payments via source_type/source_id or invoice_id (all covered by 4-branch resolver); `invoiceSync:73,119` inserts invoice_line_items just after creating the parent invoice; `useApprovalChains:76` inserts po_approval_chains with explicit `division_id: payload.division_id` (wrong division correctly rejected); `usePurchaseReturns:304-336` updates so_po_returns.status on rows user has access to; `shipments/register-tracking:130` uses `createAdminClient` service-role client (bypasses RLS). Verified 26 rows × 4 policies each via `pg_policies` post-push; zero came out permissive by accident. Ships as PR #1 of the two-PR division-switcher plan on `feature/division-switcher` branch (spec explains full plan including PR #2 with client switcher + server-enforced active division via JWT claim).
- [2026-07-30] **Fix: audit_severity enum cast in po_approval_action + log_sales_approval_decision** — `supabase/migrations/20260730100000_fix_severity_enum_cast.sql` — The 2026-07-26 pass1 enum retype changed `activity_log.severity` from `text` → `audit_severity`. Two functions insert into severity via `CASE WHEN … THEN 'critical' ELSE 'info' END` — CASE with string literals resolves to `text` and Postgres refuses the implicit text→enum cast, so the final approval step of a PO chain (triggering the "PO Fully Approved" INSERT at line 240 of the RPC) and any sales approval decision failed with `column "severity" is of type audit_severity but expression is of type text`. Bare literals in the earlier branches (`'info'`, `'warning'`, `'critical'` alone in VALUES) still work because they resolve as `unknown` and coerce to the target column type — only the CASE branches needed the cast. Fix: append `::audit_severity` to the CASE result in both functions. Bodies otherwise identical to live definitions pulled via `pg_get_functiondef`. Full-DB sweep completed (query against `pg_proc` for functions writing to any retyped enum column via CASE/COALESCE/NULLIF): 15 additional candidates all verified safe — either use explicit `::enum_name` casts on each CASE arm (`create_sale_order`, `generate_invoice_from_so`, `create_order_with_dates`, `_maybe_close_return`, `_return_resolution_status`, `cc_dedup_insert_message`), use bare literals in VALUES (`allocate_warehouse_stock`, `receive_transfer`, `allocate_landed_cost`, `submit_credit_group_change`), are read-only (`search_customers`, `rpc_customer_statement`), or write to non-enum target columns (`custom_access_token_hook` writes JWT claim; `refresh_all_stock_summaries`/`refresh_stock_summary_row` target table has no retyped enum columns). Reported by user via screenshot on PO-2026-07-015 approval; fixed and verified in the UI before commit.

- [2026-07-29] **Phase 7.7 sub-commit: rewrite `customer_credit_balances` view for dual ledger** — `supabase/migrations/20260730000600_customer_credit_balances_dual_ledger.sql` — Phase 6's view assumed a CN was 100% one resolution type and summed `cn.total_amount` where `cn.resolution_type = 'store_credit'`. Two blockers under Phase 7's dual ledger: (a) `_maybe_close_return` only stamps `'replacement'` or `'refund'`, so pure-store-credit CNs never appeared (Team 01's SR-00007 5×1,500 = QAR 7,500 was invisible); (b) `cn.total_amount` would over-count for mixed CNs — CN-00013's 900 total is really 600 refund + 300 store credit. Rewritten view derives issued amount from `return_line_customer_resolutions` where type=store_credit, summing `qty × sale_order_lines.unit_price` (return_lines has no unit_price of its own; SO line for the same brand_variant is the authoritative source). Redemption logic unchanged. Verified on staging: doc test = QAR 300 (was 0), Team 01 = QAR 7,500 (was 0).
- [2026-07-29] **Phase 7.7 sub-commit: fix write-off double stock-movement + zero cost** — `supabase/migrations/20260730000500_fix_write_off_double_movement.sql` — Two Phase 6→7 seams collided on every damaged write-off: (a) `rpc_process_return_restock` still wrote a Phase-6 `sale_return_damaged` audit marker for damaged lines at restock (proper FIFO cost, not linked to any disposition) while Phase 7's disposition RPCs also wrote their own — 2 movement rows per unit; (b) both disposition write-off paths inserted `inventory_stock_movements` without `unit_cost`, defaulting to 0, so the write-off's real cost was invisible in reports. Stock impact was zero (`sale_return_damaged` is audit-only) — reporting bug not inventory bug. Fix: restock RPC now filters inner loop to `condition = 'good'` (damaged units wait for explicit disposition per Phase 7 spec); new internal `_return_line_fifo_unit_cost` helper computes FIFO unit_cost from `cogs_entries`; `rpc_record_inventory_disposition` + `rpc_create_partial_replacement` disposition loop both stamp the FIFO cost on the movement row; one-shot backfill updated existing zero-cost linked movements and deleted orphan restock-time markers. Verified on staging: 4 rows collapsed to 2, both at 180.49.

- [2026-07-27] **Two-flow sale-return dialog: Direct + Inspection modes** (`9e3d43bd` + migration `057266eb`) — Redesigned the Create Return dialog around the operator's two real intents: **Direct return** (creator already knows good vs damaged; classify at creation time, restock immediately) and **Inspection return** (items need physical check before decision; created in a new `pending_inspection` state, triaged later). **DB (migration `20260728040000`):** ALTER TYPE `return_status` ADD VALUE `pending_inspection` BEFORE `received` (own transaction — Postgres locks the enum before commit when new labels are used elsewhere in the same txn); `rpc_process_return_restock` gains a pre-check that raises if any line is still `condition='inspection'` (belt-and-braces on top of the app status guard); new `rpc_complete_return_inspection(p_return_id, p_splits jsonb, p_restock_warehouse_id uuid)` — validates each split's line is an inspection line, that `good_qty + damaged_qty` equals the original inspection qty, and that no inspection line was omitted from the splits; deletes each inspection line and inserts replacement good/damaged rows; assigns restock warehouse; advances status → `received`. **App (commit `9e3d43bd`):** extracted CreateReturnDialog out of `SoReturnsTab` into its own file (~150 LOC of inline markup gone); added mode toggle at top; **Direct mode:** per line two qty inputs (Good + Damaged + optional damage notes) with a delivery-breakdown context row per variant showing per-warehouse `qty_delivered` from the new `useDeliveryBreakdownBySO` hook (aggregates `sale_delivery_lines JOIN sale_deliveries WHERE status='delivered'`) so the operator sees the return-qty ceiling; save expands each variant into up to two `return_lines` rows (Section 2B RPC loops per row per condition — no RPC change needed); **Inspection mode:** single qty per line, restock warehouse hidden, save writes `condition='inspection'` rows and creates the return in `pending_inspection`. New `CompleteInspectionDialog`: opens from a "Complete Inspection" button that appears on any `pending_inspection` return in `SoReturnsTab`; per inspection line, split original qty into good + damaged (mismatch surfaces inline as `+N`/`−N`), pick restock warehouse, submit calls the new `useCompleteReturnInspection` hook → new RPC. Type widening: new `SaleReturnStatus` and `ReturnLineCondition` aliases exported from the hook. `database.types.ts` hand-edited for the enum extension and new RPC (staging types-gen endpoint still unhealthy — same precedent as `feedback_supabase_gen_types.md`). Purple status pill for `pending_inspection` on both the returns list page (with `AlertTriangle` icon) and the SoReturnsTab per-return cards. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- [2026-07-27] **Hotfix 3: `create_inventory_receival` carve semantics — qty snapshot + NULL landed guard + two movement rows** (`99b1abdb`) — Trace of the carve branch surfaced three semantic bugs (none were blocking the receival call itself post-hotfix-1/2, but they distort ledger truth). **Fix 1:** `v_landed_cost := v_source_layer.landed_cost_per_unit` — if the source layer had that column NULL (nullable, default 0; no live rows have NULL today but the risk exists), then `total_unit_cost = p_unit_cost + v_landed_cost` evaluates to NULL and violates the NOT NULL constraint. Wrapped in `COALESCE(..., 0)`; also defensively COALESCEd the source's own `total_unit_cost` fallback path. **Fix 2:** the old body decremented BOTH `qty` and `remaining_qty` on the source layer. Project convention (established by `rpc_process_return_restock`, `deduct_fifo_layers`, and the recent Section 2B rewrite) treats `qty` as the ORIGINAL received amount — an immutable snapshot — while `remaining_qty` is the live counter. Decrementing `qty` on carve makes historical reports that read `qty` ("originally received N") drift below the true value. Now only `remaining_qty` moves. **Fix 3:** the old body wrote a single `qty=0` `inventory_receival_carve` movement row. Truthful about net warehouse stock (carve is intra-warehouse, intra-variant, layer-to-layer — nothing enters or leaves) but invisible to analytics filtering `qty <> 0`, and the cost transformation from source to new layer isn't visible. Now writes TWO rows: `qty=-p_qty at source.total_unit_cost` (with note "… carved out of source layer") + `qty=+p_qty at new_layer.total_unit_cost` (note "… carved into new layer"). `sum(qty)` still nets to zero (net-zero warehouse impact preserved) but both endpoints of the carve are individually auditable. `new_stock` branch keeps its single `+p_qty` row unchanged — that's a genuine warehouse-level stock increase. Migration `20260728030000` is a full CREATE OR REPLACE (cleaner than three regex swaps); preserves hotfix 1 (`receival_id` no `::text`) and hotfix 2 (`v_movement_type::stock_movement_type`) verbatim. **Verified end-to-end by calling the real RPC inside a RAISE-EXCEPTION rollback with a session whose `auth.uid()` was bound to a receiver-role user via `set_config('request.jwt.claims',…)`:** source qty 5→5 (preserved ✓), source remaining 5→3 (decremented ✓), new layer `total_unit_cost = 700` (COALESCE path exercised ✓), OUT movement `qty=-2 uc=500` (source cost ✓), IN movement `qty=+2 uc=700` (new cost ✓), `sum(mvt.qty) = 0` (net-zero ✓).
- [2026-07-27] **Hotfix 2: `create_inventory_receival` — enum-cast `v_movement_type`** (`e632df03`) — Second bug in the same RPC, surfaced only after hotfix 1 unblocked step 3. Step 8 (stock movement insert) writes `v_movement_type text` into `inventory_stock_movements.movement_type` which is the native enum `stock_movement_type` — Postgres rejects the implicit text→enum coercion with `42804: column "movement_type" is of type stock_movement_type but expression is of type text`. Migration `20260728020000` regex-swaps the insert VALUES site to add `::stock_movement_type`, anchored on surrounding text so the DECLARE and assignments are untouched. Verified via the full six-step DO-block simulation running to completion inside a RAISE-EXCEPTION rollback (`ROLLBACK_ALL_OK` fires past step 8). **Lesson:** an RPC that failed at step 3 was masking step 8's bug; when a hotfix "unblocks" a chain, keep testing until the whole flow runs.
- [2026-07-27] **Hotfix: `create_inventory_receival` — stale `::text` cast on `receival_id`** (`16dbbda1`) — UI reported "Failed to create receival" for every add-new-stock and carve-from-stock attempt from the Inventory → Create Receival dialog. Repro via a step-by-step DO block surfaced the actual error: `42804: column "receival_id" is of type uuid but expression is of type text`. The RPC's `INSERT INTO fifo_cost_layers (..., receival_id, ...) VALUES (..., v_new_receival.id::text, ...)` was casting the receival uuid to text before inserting into a uuid column. `fifo_cost_layers.receival_id` was retyped to uuid at some point and the RPC wasn't swept. Migration `20260728010000` fixes it via the same `pg_get_functiondef` + regex-replace pattern used in `20260725110005` / `20260727090000`, stripping `v_new_receival.id::text` down to `v_new_receival.id` in both branches. Post-fix, the six-step simulation (insert receivals → receival_items → fifo layer → bump stock_level → stock movement → recalc_average_cost) runs to completion inside a RAISE-EXCEPTION rollback. **Process note:** this cast wasn't caught by the earlier sweeps because it wasn't a table-rename issue — it was a column-type drift. Future column retype migrations should be paired with a grep of `pg_proc.prosrc` for `<column>::` casts of the wrong type.
- [2026-07-27] **Section 2B: Sale-return restock — reverse exact FIFO/COGS + track damaged units** (`055b534b`) — `rpc_process_return_restock` was broken (referenced non-existent `v_return.items` JSONB column, regressed by the 2026-07-25 `returns → so_po_returns` regex sweep which was fed by a stale `pg_get_functiondef` snapshot from before the JSONB→`return_lines` rewrite). Full rewrite per user choice — Option C + damaged tracking. Migration `20260728000000`. **Good branch:** walks per-layer `cogs_entries` rows for the `(sale_order_id, brand_variant_id)` in FIFO consumption order (`date, unit_cost, id`) — thanks to Section 10 these exist one-per-consumed-layer. For each source layer, takes `min(cogs.qty, qty_remaining)` and: inserts a new `fifo_cost_layers` row at `cogs.unit_cost` (source_type='sale_return', source_id=return_id); inserts a **reversing** `cogs_entries` row (qty=-N, total=-N×unit_cost, notes='Reversed by return &lt;return_number&gt;'); inserts one `inventory_stock_movements` row per source-layer chunk. Bumps `stock_level` by total good qty, calls `recalc_average_cost`. **Damaged branch (condition ≠ 'good'):** same per-layer walk to preserve historical cost accuracy, but writes only `inventory_stock_movements` rows with `movement_type='sale_return_damaged'` at the ORIGINAL sale unit_cost per source layer. No FIFO layer, no `stock_level` change, no COGS reversal — damaged units visible in the ledger without touching valuation. Guardrails: `FOR UPDATE` lock, idempotency via `restocked_at IS NOT NULL`, precondition `status='restocked'`, `source_type='sale_order'` enforced, raises if return qty exceeds sum of matched cogs qty for a line. **Staging verify (BEGIN…ROLLBACK via DO block + RAISE-EXCEPTION):** SR-00002 (damaged, 3 units of variant `cd95755a…` at cogs=2.53) → stock_level 40→40 unchanged, +1 movement `sale_return_damaged` at 2.53, +0 layers, +0 COGS reversals ✓; SR-00001 (good, 5 units of variant `9d8c0d6a…` — 80-gal heater — with single pre-Section-10 cogs row at 795.81) → stock_level 89→94, +1 fifo layer at 795.81, +1 reversing cogs (qty=-5, total=-3979.06), +1 movement at 795.81, avg_cost recalculated ✓. Multi-COGS-row (post-Section-10 sales) exercises the same `FOR v_cogs IN … LOOP` — same code path, more iterations. Follow-up: the ideal true multi-layer end-to-end demo still needs a POST-Section-10 sale of a multi-cost variant (only the 80-gal heater qualifies today, and its historical sale predates Section 10). `docs/next-work-plan.md` Section 2B: 🔍 → ✅.
- [2026-07-27] **Two hotfixes for Section 10 + Scenario 2A staging verify** (`063c6814`) — Section 10 shipped with two problems that surfaced during the 2A verify run. **Hotfix 1** (`20260727080000`): the new `RETURNS TABLE(… source_type, source_id …)` OUT parameters shadowed columns of the same name on `fifo_cost_layers`, causing Postgres 42702 `column reference "source_type" is ambiguous` on every deduct call. Aliased the table (`fcl`) and renamed the RECORD fields (`r_source_type` / `r_source_id`). **Hotfix 2** (`20260727090000`): the Section 10 rewrites were authored from `baseline_schema.sql` which still referenced the OLD table name `inventory_brand_variants` — renamed to `inventory_item_brand_variants` in `20260724180001` and the compat view since dropped. My rewrites regressed 5 functions (`deduct_fifo_layers`, `complete_delivery_inventory`, `approve_stock_adjustment_inventory`, `allocate_warehouse_stock`, `rpc_process_po_return_dispatch`). Same regex-swap-and-EXECUTE pass used in `20260725140001` — rewrote all 7 offending functions (also caught 2 pre-existing: `apply_adjustment`, `rpc_process_return_restock` that the July 25 sweep missed) in one atomic loop. Pure name-swap, zero semantic change. **2A verify result:** `deduct_fifo_layers(bv, wh, 10)` against a real 2-layer variant (80-gallon heater — L1: 8 remaining @ 1363.21; L2: 5 remaining @ 436.23) returned 2 rows: `(L1, 8, 1363.21, 10905.68)` + `(L2, 2, 436.23, 872.46)`. Per-layer breakdown correct; FIFO ordering correct (oldest drained first); total qty 10 ✓. Test wrapped in `BEGIN…ROLLBACK` — layers' `remaining_qty` verified preserved after rollback. **Process note:** future function-body rewrites need to source from the LIVE DB via `pg_get_functiondef`, not `baseline_schema.sql` — the baseline is a frozen snapshot and doesn't reflect subsequent renames. Adding this to the working-style memory.
- [2026-07-27] **Per-layer FIFO breakdown for downstream ledgers** (`2a88e334`) — Section 10 (subsumes Scenario 2A). Trace of the sale-delivery path exposed that `deduct_fifo_layers` returned only aggregated `(total_cost, weighted_unit_cost)` — so every caller (delivery, SA approval, dispatch, PO return, allocate, transfer) wrote a single collapsed row into `cogs_entries` / `inventory_stock_movements` even when the draw spanned multiple layers. Scenario 2A (R1 5@10 + R2 5@12 delivery of 10) got 1 COGS row @ 11.00 instead of the expected 2 rows (5@10, 5@12). Same root cause as Section 10, so both sections closed in one migration. Migration `20260727070000` retypes `deduct_fifo_layers` return to per-layer `(layer_id, source_type, source_id, qty_taken, unit_cost, total_cost)` (DROP + CREATE — signature change), then rewrites all 6 live callers: `complete_delivery_inventory` (N COGS + N movements per line — fixes 2A end-to-end), `approve_stock_adjustment_inventory` (N movements on decrease/damage/write_off branches — increase branch untouched), `dispatch_transfer` (N transfer_out per item — preserves the `ORDER BY brand_variant_id` deadlock guard), `rpc_process_po_return_dispatch` (N purchase_return per line — same pass swaps `FROM returns` compat view → canonical `so_po_returns` from 20260724160002, one less view consumer), `allocate_warehouse_stock` (decrease branch N movements at real layer costs — also fixes pre-existing bug where the single movement used caller-supplied `p_unit_cost` instead of drained layer costs; increase branch verbatim), `receive_transfer` (option (i) — walks the transfer_out movements in dispatch order = FIFO source order, iterates them consuming received_qty layer-by-layer; each dispatched layer becomes either a full destination layer + transfer_in at the source cost, a split partial layer + transfer_shrinkage both at the layer's cost, or a pure transfer_shrinkage — over-receipt clamped to preserve current behaviour). Pre-flight cleared 3 non-callers: `approve_warehouse_transfer_inventory` already dropped, `apply_adjustment` / `inventory_adjustments` dead (queued for future cleanup), `apply_inventory_check_adjustments` no longer calls deduct (Section 1.18 rewrite). `database.types.ts` `deduct_fifo_layers.Returns` retyped to the new shape (no app code calls the RPC directly — grep confirmed). Reversibility: pure logic change on CREATE OR REPLACE plus a single DROP+CREATE — no schema alters, no backfill; historical rows from before this migration remain aggregated. Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean. Staging verify pending — run Scenario 2A (R1 5@10 + R2 100@12, deliver 10), expect 2 COGS rows + 2 movement rows. `receive_transfer` shrinkage attribution now costs missing units to the specific source layers they came from rather than a blended cost.

Both skipped the entire `stock_adjustment_approvals` chain — meaning any warehouse RP could push an SA through in one click, defeating the strict per-step role gating that migration `20260726140000` was explicitly built to enforce (that migration's own comment: "removing Admin bypass — defeats the whole per-role chain"). Missed it in yesterday's PROGRESS write-up. - [2026-07-27] **Wire `user_started` event for inventory-check assignments** (`63c9ca62`) — Section 1.12. `inventory_check_assignments.started_at` was declared but never written; the intended state machine `pending → in_progress → completed` was missing its middle transition. Assignments jumped straight `pending → completed`, so the UI's "Counting" badge and `in_progress` filters were rendering a state that never actually happened. Log had no per-user "started counting" event either. Migration `20260727010000` extends `save_inventory_check_item_count` RPC with optional assignment context. On every count-save it idempotently transitions the caller's assignment `pending → in_progress`, stamps `started_at = now()`, and inserts a `'user_started'` row into `inventory_check_log` — all inside one function so log + column can't drift. The gate is `WHERE status='pending'`, so subsequent saves are no-ops on the transition side (the UPDATE's `RETURNING check_id INTO v_check_id` is null on second-and-later calls, gating the log insert). App: `useSaveItemCount` accepts optional `assignmentId` / `profileId` / `profileName` and passes to the RPC; onSuccess also invalidates the log + assignments query keys so the Timeline picks up the fresh event and the badge flips. `CountingPanel.handleSave` passes context only on the writable path (`readOnly=false`) — admin/read-only views can't trigger a spurious transition. Timeline: `EventIcon` + `eventLabel` gain a `'user_started'` case (blue clock icon, "Started counting"). Duplication audit confirmed the pattern is intentional log-plus-cached-columns, not accidental duplication. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- [2026-07-27] **Drop `inventory_categories.description`** (`021ab56b`) — Section 1.11. Column was added in July as "internal notes" but the only reader was the same edit dialog that wrote it — no display of the description anywhere else. User's call: name + type already communicate purpose. `updated_at` kept explicitly per user request for audit-trail integrity (no trigger maintains it today but the column preserves the option to add one later without a schema migration). Migration `20260727000000` drops the column. App: `CategoryEditDialog` loses the description state / pre-fill / payload key / JSX Textarea block / unused Textarea import; `useInventory` drops description from both category mutation payload types; `database.types.ts` Row/Insert/Update slimmed. `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Process note:** on this one I skipped the analysis-first step the user asked for and executed the migration before confirming. User pulled me back — future column drops in Section 1 require a full column audit + green light before any DDL.
- [2026-07-27] **Add `fifo_cost_layers.source_id` for full source traceability** (`4f6fa59e`) — Section 1.10. Only receival-sourced layers had a back-link before (`receival_id` FK); other layers only carried the `source_type` tag, so `FifoLayersTable` rendered sale-return layers as "Manual". Migration `20260726260000` adds a nullable polymorphic `source_id` uuid column (no FK — target table depends on source_type, mirrors `inventory_stock_movements.reference_id`), backfills receival layers from `receival_id` directly and other types via a same-second join against `inventory_stock_movements` (clamped to enum-valid `movement_type`s: sale_return, purchase_return, adjustment, transfer_in, inventory_check). Rewrites `rpc_process_return_restock` to set `source_id = p_return_id` on new sale_return layers (also picks up the `returns → so_po_returns` rename that was still lingering in the fn body) and `apply_adjustment` to set `source_id = p_adjustment_id` on new increase layers. App: `useInventory` FIFO select + `FifoLayer` type + `database.types.ts` all gain `source_id`; `FifoLayersTable` adds explicit `sale_return` and `po_return` cases (emerald / red) so the "Manual" fallback now catches only truly unclassifiable layers. Follow-up (not this task): resolve `(source_type, source_id)` → click-through target in the UI. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- [2026-07-26] **`debit_notes` cleanup + 2 FKs** (`e450163f`) — Section 1.9. Mirror of the credit_notes treatment from 1.5. Migration `20260726250000` drops 5 dead columns (`approved_by`, `phone`, `type`, `notes`, `created_by` — same story as credit_notes: no writers/readers, `phone` was PDF-only and migrated to a suppliers join) and adds 2 FKs backfilled with a sync trigger: `reason_id` → `reason_lists(id)` (case-insensitive label match, prefer `category='po_return'`; silent-fallback sync trigger keeps reason text as historical snapshot); `supplier_id` → `suppliers(id)` (backfilled via `bill_id` → `bills.supplier_id` first, fallback via `purchase_order_id` → `purchase_orders.supplier_id` because `bill_id` is often NULL on auto-generated notes). `bill_id` kept nullable — a PO return can legitimately happen before the bill is created. First had to rewrite `debit_notes_invalidate_pdf_cache_fn` to drop its `OLD.notes` reference before dropping the notes column (trigger fires on UPDATE and would fail with "record OLD has no field notes"). App: `usePurchaseReturns.createDebitNoteForReturn` stops sending `type:'auto'`, PO fetch now selects `supplier_id`, debit note insert fills `supplier_id` from PO. `generate-credit-debit-note-pdf.ts` swaps `note.phone` for a `suppliers(phone)` join. Types slimmed (`DebitNote`, `database.types.ts` Row/Insert/Update, Relationships block). `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- [2026-07-26] **Drop unused `debit_note_lines.bill_line_id`** (`5a2d9b00`) — Section 1.8. FK was scaffolded in the July split but no writer was ever wired up — every row NULL, no reader, no RPC join. Full column-by-column audit confirmed everything else on `debit_note_lines` is active. User's "unit cost when a return spans two receivals" concern turned out to be a column-name confusion: `debit_note_lines.unit_price` is the supplier-price snapshot (drives the debit total); the FIFO-weighted cost across receivals is separately maintained on `inventory_stock_movements.unit_cost` via `rpc_process_po_return_dispatch` → `deduct_fifo_layers`. Two concepts, both correct. Migration `20260726240000` drops the FK constraint then the column. Types + `DebitNoteLine` slimmed. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

Migration `20260726210000` drops 5 dead columns (`approved_by`, `phone`, `type`, `notes`, `created_by` — all confirmed no writers/readers except `phone` which was PDF-only, migrated to customer FK join) and adds 3 FKs backfilled with sync triggers per Pass 2c pattern: `reason_id` → `reason_lists(id)` (case-insensitive label match, prefer `category='sale_return'`), `customer_id` → `customers(id)` (backfilled via `invoice_id` → `so_invoices.customer_id` first, fallback via `source_return_id` → `so_po_returns` → `sale_orders.customer_id`), `refund_method_id` → `payment_methods(id)` (slug match). `customer_id` kept nullable — return-before-invoice case can leave the chain unresolvable for old rows. - [2026-07-26] **Bills PDF cache wiring + drop `tax`/`updated_at`** (`4986263a`) — Section 1.4 follow-up. Extended audit surfaced 4 more candidates; user opted to KEEP `pdf_url` + `needs_refresh` and wire them up as a real PDF cache mirroring the AR-side pattern from `20260627101900_sales_pdf_cache`. Migration `20260726200000` drops `tax` (always 0) and `updated_at` (no writer/reader); adds `bills_invalidate_pdf_cache_fn` (BEFORE UPDATE trigger: nulls `pdf_url` and sets `needs_refresh=true` on any bill update unless `app.skip_pdf_invalidation` GUC is `'true'`); cascade fn from `bill_line_items`; and `set_bill_pdf_url(uuid, text)` RPC (SECURITY DEFINER, sets the GUC then writes URL + clears the flag). Payment invalidation is implicit — payments/allocations already fire `bill_recompute_paid_fn` which UPDATEs the bill, and that UPDATE now flows through the invalidation trigger. Once `payment_status='paid'` there are no more payments, so `needs_refresh` stays false forever after the final regen — matches the user's explicit desired behavior. App: `generate-bill-pdf.ts` gains cache short-circuit (return cached `pdf_url` when `!needs_refresh` and no `force`), then persists the fresh URL via the new RPC; return type gains `regenerated: boolean`. `useSupplierBills` stops sending `tax:0`. `Bill` type slimmed. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- [2026-07-26] **Inventory Check → Stock Adjustment auto-generation (Option B)** (`cba1dfa8`) — approved inventory checks no longer mutate FIFO/stock directly. `apply_inventory_check_adjustments` rewritten to loop non-zero-variance lines and generate one `stock_adjustments` row per variance (status=pending_approval), each linked via new `source_check_id` + `source_check_item_id` FK columns. Each SA rides its own approval chain (built from `approval_workflow_steps` for workflow='stock_adj'); actual stock movement happens only on the SA's final approval via existing `approve_stock_adjustment_inventory`. Variance→type map: `+ → increase`; `− + damage → damage`; `− + write_off → write_off`; `− + null → decrease`. Requester attributed to the check's top approver. UI: new "Adjustments" tab on the check detail (only rendered when `generatedSAs.length > 0`) listing item/type/qty/created/status with type-tinted badges; primary-tinted banner on the SA detail dialog when `source_check_id` is set showing the source check number. Rejection behavior locked in as isolate-per-SA (rejected SA doesn't reopen parent check). Backfill deliberately skipped for existing approved checks. Types stale (dev project paused, can't regen), so hooks cast through `unknown` — runtime correct, types realign on next regen. Migration `20260726120000`, applied to staging.

B enum values: `increase, decrease, set, damage, write_off` — matches both `CreateAdjustmentPayload` and `CreateAdjustmentV2Payload` writers in `useWarehouseOperations` plus the baseline PL/pgSQL branch. C enum values: `initialized, user_completed, all_counted, approval_action, approved, rejected` — the "vocabulary decision" the spec worried about was a non-issue; all 6 writers in `useWarehouseOperations` and both readers (`EventIcon` + `eventLabel` in `WhInventoryCheckDetail`) already agree. Both migrations are pre-flight guard + CREATE TYPE + single ALTER (no CHECK / index / default to disturb). Applied to staging (`mwvblpgbgxipvrevkeff`) via `supabase db push`; smoke-tested with `tsc --noEmit`. Migrations `20260726070000`, `20260726080000`. Pattern-time: ~10 min combined.

Setter triggers (`sync_invoice_id_column`, `invoice_paid_amount_sync`, `split_invoices_into_bills`) keep working because PL/pgSQL implicitly casts text literals to enum. Nullability preserved. Migration `20260726060000`. Pattern-time so far: Pilot A (po_edit_requests.status) with vocabulary rename + 2 index rebuilds = ~30 min; Pilot D (payments.source_type) with no writer touch = ~15 min.

- [2026-07-26] **Store credit redeemable on SO + invoice payments** (`e0edca64`) — customers holding open store-credit balances (credit_notes.resolution_type='store_credit') can now pay SO/invoice balances with that credit before falling back to a real payment method. New RPC-less flow: `useApplyStoreCredit` batches one payment row per CN consumed (FIFO across the customer's open CNs, method='store_credit', credit_note_id linked). `payments.credit_note_id` FK added; `customer_credit_balances` view subtracts applied payments so remaining balance is derived (no drift). SoPaymentDialog + CustomerPaymentDialog both show an emerald "Apply store credit — QAR X available" checkbox with editable amount; PaymentFormDialog gained `headerSlot` / `outstandingOverride` / `extraConfirmationLines` props to host the panel and hide the Cash method when credit covers the full remaining. Kept independent of credit-limit math per user's design call. Also fixed while wiring: `useOpenCreditNotesForCustomer` was 400ing because it embedded sale_orders through the polymorphic `so_po_returns.source_id` FK (batched separately now); credit_notes and debit_notes list queries embedded the dropped `returns` compat view (swapped to `so_po_returns`); `useResolveCreditNoteStoreCredit` no longer hard-fails when the CN has no invoice_id (falls back through the return chain).
- [2026-07-25] **Credit balance columns on Suppliers + Customers** (`86f557be`) — new "Credit Balance" column on Master Data → Suppliers, new "We Owe" column on Master Data → Customers. Emerald badges show per-currency open balances (multi-currency: one badge per currency, e.g. `USD 150.00 · QAR 25.00`). Click opens a shared `CreditBalanceDialog` listing source DNs / CNs grouped by currency, each row a `target="_blank"` link to the DN/CN detail. Backed by two computed views (`supplier_credit_balances` / `customer_credit_balances`) so no denormalized column can drift when a note status flips. Debit-notes and credit-notes list pages now honour `?dn=<id>` / `?cn=<id>` query params — auto-open the detail dialog for deep-linking from the popup. Read-only for now: doesn't affect `customer_credit_used` or SO payment flow — those two integrations are queued follow-ups per user's plan.
- [2026-07-25] **SO edit re-approval flow** (`559526c1`) — new RPC `apply_sale_order_edit` replaces the plain UPDATE that used to run on SO edits. Recomputes totals, rebalances reserved stock via `batch_update_reserved_qty` deltas, re-runs the credit + margin checks, and flips the SO back to `pending_approval` + builds a fresh sales approval chain when a threshold is crossed (idempotent — any prior pending chain rows get marked `Superseded by SO edit` first). `useUpdateSO` routes line-item / discount edits through the RPC; metadata-only edits (terms, validity days, exchange rate, notes) stay on the direct UPDATE. Edit page toast surfaces the resulting status. Mirrors how POs work; closes `task_5012314b`.
- [2026-07-25] **Drop 3 compat views + rewrite ~40 dependent functions** (`d35de0d7`) — 13 migrations. Views `profiles` / `returns` / `inventory_brand_variants` are gone. Phase 1-6 per plan plus a large sweep pass that caught 20+ inventory functions and 8 auth/storage helpers the plan's grep missed. Latent bugs also patched: `resubmit_sale_order` referenced dropped `customer_type` column; `force_approve_credit_group_change` referenced dropped `customer_type` + `is_blocked`; several `LANGUAGE sql` functions (`has_admin_permission`, `custom_access_token_hook`, storage RLS helpers) still referenced `cr.is_system` (renamed to `is_system_admin` in 20260724160000, never propagated). Client-side fixes shipped in same commit: `p_expected_delivery` sent `null` instead of `''` for date; `useSaleReturns` resolves `user_data.id` from `auth_user_id` for the FK; DN dedup + partial unique index + client idempotency guard on `usePurchaseReturns.createDebitNoteForReturn` (real dupes DN-00003 auto-deleted); PO return dialog reason swapped to `useReturnReasons('po_return')` select; SO line-item Add row swapped Tools & Assets for Spare Parts.
- [2026-07-25] **Credit-Group Approval consolidation + Pending Dialog** (`bddc0b46`) — `src/app/(dashboard)/master-data/customers/page.tsx`, `src/components/master-data/CreditGroupPendingDialog.tsx` (new), `src/hooks/useCreditGroupApprovals.ts`, `src/hooks/useCreditGroups.ts`, 1 migration. Scope re-scoped from the original ~1-day spec after review: the Edit Customer dialog was already routing limit-bearing group changes through `submit_credit_group_change` (approval chain) — the redundant "Assign group…" dropdown on the customers list was the actual problem because it skipped the doc-upload gate. Killed that column entirely; list now shows plain group name + amber "Pending: <group>" pill for any customer with an in-flight request. Pill is clickable → new `CreditGroupPendingDialog` shows the full approval chain (per-step approver, timestamps, comments/reason), "Waiting on X" hint, and a two-step Cancel Request flow. New RPC `cancel_credit_group_change(p_request_id, p_reason)` — requester or approver may withdraw; marks steps rejected, unblocks customer if auto-blocked, logs activity. Cleanup: `useAssignCreditGroup` direct-write mutation removed (zero callers). Skipped from the original spec (redundant with existing flow): dedicated `CreditGroupRequestDialog`, storage helper extract, `useCreditGroupRequest` combined hook, feature flag, Owner force-assign menu (already available on the Approvals page).
- [2026-07-25] **Tool Serial Tracking Phase 2: Placeholder confirm UI + post-receival step** (`4c6a63f8`) — `src/components/services/inventory/PlaceholderUnitRow.tsx` (new), `src/components/purchase/ReceivalSerialsStep.tsx` (new), `ToolCategoryRow.tsx`, `PoReceiveTab.tsx`, `PoDetailDialog.tsx`, `useInventory.ts`, 2 migrations. Placeholder tool_asset_units (auto-created by DB triggers on tools-category receivals) now render as inline rows with Serial / Brand / Expiry inputs + amber "pending serial" pill + Confirm button that flips is_placeholder=false. Per-item "Auto-generate N serials" button calls `auto_generate_tool_serials` RPC. After every PO receival, a `ReceivalSerialsStep` dialog opens at the PoDetailDialog level (so it survives the Receive tab unmounting on status flip) listing new placeholders grouped by item. DB fix: `auto_generate_tool_serials` also flips `is_placeholder=false` (was only writing serial_number, leaving flag stuck true); backfill migration corrects rows already written by the broken RPC. UI pending rule made defensive: filters on "no serial yet" rather than the flag.
- [2026-07-24] **Inventory tree: show direct items on non-leaf categories** (`d1942e72`) — `src/components/services/inventory/CategoryRow.tsx`, `src/components/services/inventory/ToolCategoryRow.tsx` — both files gated the item query on `isLeaf = node.children.length === 0`, so any category with even one sub-category never fetched or rendered its direct items. Categories like `Test for new Mitigation` holding both a sub-category and a direct item hid the latter entirely. Fixed by un-gating the item fetch + render + Add-Item button + item edit dialog. RPC (`get_category_stock_aggregates`) was already correct — variant_count included these items all along, the bug was purely display. Category rollup verification task done.
- [2026-07-24] **UI polish + planning specs queued** (`96612689`) — `src/components/shared/PaymentConfirmationDialog.tsx` — bumped DialogFooter gap-2 → gap-3, added min-w-24 to Go Back and min-w-40 font-semibold to Confirm Payment so the two CTAs no longer sit hip-to-hip. Also queued 4 planning docs under `docs/specs/`: drop-compat-views (rewrite 27 stored functions + drop 3 compat views), credit-group-request-dialog (~1 day UI rewrite to replace direct-write dropdown with proper approval+doc-gate flow), inventory-division-denormalization Phase 2-4, tool-serial-tracking Phase 2-4.
- [2026-07-24] **Tool serial tracking Phase 1 + inventory division_id Phase 1 + receival_id cast fixes** (`1c416334`) — 8 migrations. **Tool serial:** `tool_asset_units` gets `receival_item_id` FK + `is_placeholder` boolean; AFTER INSERT trigger on `fifo_cost_layers` auto-creates N placeholder units for tools-category receivals with `serial_number=NULL` (user enters real serials); symmetric AFTER DELETE trigger removes still-placeholder units on receival cancel; new RPC `auto_generate_tool_serials(item_id)` fills NULLs with `<sku>-<3-digit-ordinal>` as escape hatch. **Division denormalization:** nullable `division_id UUID` added to `receivals`, `receival_items`, `fifo_cost_layers`, `inventory_stock_movements`, `cogs_entries`, `warehouse_transfers`; 4 trigger functions + 6 BEFORE INSERT triggers auto-populate from source columns; backfill runs in same txn. **Receival RPC bug fix:** `create_and_approve_receival` + `apply_receival_edit` were writing `v_receival_id::TEXT` to `fifo_cost_layers.receival_id` — the column was migrated to `uuid` at some point (probably `20260715100000_normalize_schema`) but the cast wasn't updated. Every PO receival crashed with "column receival_id is of type uuid but expression is of type text". Fixed. `approve_receival_inventory` already correct (writes uuid directly).
- [2026-07-24] **Enum tightening + drop redundant columns** (`a986a592`) — 4 migrations. `po_versions.stage` text+CHECK → proper `po_stage` enum. `bills` drops `doc_status` entirely (approval workflow was never wired into UI; `useApproveBill` hook had zero callers; aging filter `.neq('doc_status','rejected')` never fired). `customers.entity_type` text+CHECK → `customer_entity_type` enum. `customers.is_blocked` dropped — derivable from `block_reason IS NOT NULL` (same redundancy pattern the earlier `customer_type` drop already fixed); `customer_credit_summary` view + `search_customers` RPC now derive it. Rewrote the 3 credit-approval RPCs (`submit_credit_group_change`, `approve_credit_group_change`, `reject_credit_group_change`) to (a) remove writes to now-dropped `is_blocked`, (b) remove writes to already-dropped `customer_type` — those RPCs were silently broken since 2026-07-24 batch B, (c) switch `profiles` refs to `user_data`. App cleanup: removed `Bill.doc_status` field + `DocStatus` type + all consumers (badge, aging filter, PDF select, insert default, approval hook); removed dead `Customer.customer_type` + optimistic-cache field. Regen `database.types.ts`.

 — Task 3 (`ba3ae14e`): reset `so_invoices.manually_paid = false`, cleared cached PDFs, ran backfill via `recalculate_ar_invoice_payment_status`; no UI toggle existed to remove. - [2026-06-27] **Corrections Task: PO approval Owner-blocked fix + Customer credit-group approval coverage** — `src/hooks/usePOApprovals.ts`, `src/app/(dashboard)/purchase/approvals/page.tsx`, `src/components/purchase/PoDetailDialog.tsx`, `src/components/master-data/CustomerDialog.tsx`, `src/lib/workflow-conditions.ts`, `src/components/master-data/ApprovalChainConfig.tsx`, `src/hooks/useWorkflowSteps.ts`, `supabase/migrations/20260627120000_widen_workflow_allowlist_for_credit_group.sql`. (1) **Owner couldn't approve POs** — `useMyApprovalRoles` was slugifying role names (`'Owner'` → `'owner'`) but `po_approvals.role` had been converted to capitalised human-readable names in migration `20260615131936`. The mismatch meant `myRoles.includes(step.role)` never matched, so even the Owner saw the "you don't hold any of the pending roles" warning. Hook now returns role names verbatim; Force-Approve button and PoDetailDialog Edit/Recall gates re-pointed to `'Owner'`. (2) **Customer credit-group approval coverage** — New customer creation and the edit dialog wrote `credit_group_id` directly, bypassing the workflow added in commit 0974c7e. Both paths now route through `submit_credit_group_change` whenever the picked group has a non-zero credit limit. For Create the customer is inserted with `credit_group_id = null` first and the group is submitted for approval; for Edit, all other fields save directly but the new group is sent for approval. Cash/zero-limit groups still assign directly. (3) **Configurable credit-group approvers** — `'credit_group'` added to `WorkflowKey`, `WORKFLOWS`, and the `add_workflow_step` / `add_workflow_step_for_role` RPC allow-lists so admins can edit who approves credit-group changes from the Approval Workflows page. (4) **Force-approve already-approved preservation** — confirmed unchanged: `useForceApproveAllSteps` only touches pending+active steps, so prior approvers stay attributed.

- [2026-06-27] **Corrections Task: Sales PDFs → HTML + Puppeteer pipeline** — `supabase/migrations/20260627101900_sales_pdf_cache.sql`, `20260627101901_sales_pdf_cache_rpc.sql`, `src/lib/pdf/html-to-pdf.ts`, `src/lib/sales/quotation-pdf-html.ts`, `src/lib/sales/generate-quotation-pdf.ts`, `src/lib/sales/invoice-pdf-html.ts`, `src/lib/sales/generate-invoice-pdf.ts`, `src/lib/sales/credit-debit-note-pdf-html.ts`, `src/lib/sales/generate-credit-debit-note-pdf.ts`, `src/app/api/sales/so/[id]/quotation-pdf/route.ts`, `src/app/api/sales/invoices/[id]/pdf/route.ts`, `src/app/api/sales/credit-notes/[id]/pdf/route.ts`, `src/components/sales/SoPdfButton.tsx`, `src/components/sales/InvoicePdfButton.tsx`, `src/components/sales/CreditDebitNoteDownloadButton.tsx`, `src/components/sales/SoDetailDialog.tsx`, `next.config.ts` (removed `transpilePackages: ['@react-pdf/renderer']` + uninstalled the package). Migrated SO Quotation / AR Invoice / Credit-Debit Note PDFs off `@react-pdf/renderer` (which crashed with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'hasOwnProperty')` on Next 15 because Next ships React 19-canary which renamed `__SECRET_INTERNALS_…` → `__CLIENT_INTERNALS_…`). New flow mirrors the order-confirmation pipeline: builders emit self-contained HTML with inlined Infield fonts + Company logo, shared `htmlToPdfBuffer()` renders via headless Chromium, generators upload to per-document public Supabase Storage buckets (`quotation-pdfs`, `invoice-pdfs`, `credit-note-pdfs`) and persist the URL on the parent row. Cache invalidation via BEFORE-UPDATE triggers on `sale_orders` / `invoices` / `credit_notes` plus AFTER-write cascades on `sale_order_lines` / `invoice_line_items` — the URL writer uses service-role RPCs (`set_sale_order_pdf_url` / `set_invoice_pdf_url` / `set_credit_note_pdf_url`) that set `app.skip_pdf_invalidation` GUC to skip the trigger only on cache-back-writes. Each button now exposes two actions: View (`window.open` the cached public URL) and Download (fetch the blob and trigger a save). The three legacy `*Inner` + `*Pdf` React-PDF components were deleted and `@react-pdf/renderer` is uninstalled.

- [2026-06-25] **PO Phase D — Edit Request Workflow for non-approvers** — `supabase/migrations/20260625130000_po_edit_requests.sql` (new `po_edit_requests` table with status enum pending/approved/declined/used, partial unique index ensuring at most one approved-unused per PO, RLS: anyone selects, self-inserts, approval-slot roles update), `src/lib/queryKeys.ts` (poEditRequests namespace), `src/hooks/usePoEditRequests.ts` (new file — 4 hooks: usePoEditRequest reads open request per PO; useCreateEditRequest validates 10-char reason, inserts, notifies all approval-slot users, logs activity; useReviewEditRequest applies decision with auto-supersede of prior approved-unused via UPDATE…WHERE status='approved' before applying, notifies requester, logs activity with appropriate severity; useMarkEditRequestUsed flips approved-unused to used), `src/hooks/usePurchaseOrders.ts` (useSubmitPoVersion now closes the unlock by flipping any approved po_edit_request to used at the tail of the mutation, wrapped in try/catch so a request-table failure doesn't undo the committed amend; onSuccess invalidates poEditRequests.byPo for instant banner removal), `src/components/purchase/RequestEditDialog.tsx` (new — small Dialog with reason Textarea, live char count, 10-char minimum gate), `src/components/purchase/EditRequestBanner.tsx` (new — inline amber-pending banner with Approve/Decline buttons gated to approval-slot roles; green approved-unused banner; Decline path opens AlertDialog for mandatory comment), `src/components/purchase/PoDetailDialog.tsx` (wired both new components; relaxed Phase C Edit gate so the Edit button appears for ANY authenticated user when an approved-unused request exists, not just Owner). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-25-po-phase-d-edit-request-workflow-design.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-25-po-phase-d-edit-request-workflow.md`.

No DB migration — the existing `useSubmitPoVersion` mutation already handles the amend mechanics (snapshot, status flip, chain reset, notifications).
